# Requirements — release chain (cut, gate reading, brief, QA coordination, GO, publish merge, closure, release note)

**Area key:** `release`. **Source of truth read:** AgentForge `origin/main` @ `f30f3699` (read-only, via git plumbing). **Target:** the Rust crate `spl-asdlc` ("the bridge"), a process in the user's environment that polls GitHub on a tick, writes to a tracker through the `Tracker` trait (Linear or Livela), and notifies through a `Notifier` trait. Config is `spl-asdlc.toml` per repository, merged with its optional instance overlay on the bridge's host (spec 08).

## Scope

This document is the contract for the release chain from the **cut** (a GitHub `workflow_dispatch` that creates the release branch and the first RC tag) to the **closure** (every embarked ticket judged, the release item closed, the release note sent). It covers:

- the cut rules of `scripts/cut-rc.sh` (branch, one cycle in flight, annotated RC tag carrying the class, tag immutability, waiting for release-please's PR, reading the version from its body);
- the candidate range, release-note coverage, per-product release shape (data), multi-component PR parsing (`release_range.py`, `release_notes.py`, `release_shape.py`, `release_cycle.py`);
- the release item (issue) lifecycle in `release_service.py`: open (idempotent, fail-closed), class, brief, GO (`evaluate_go`, `GoDecision`, `blockers_carried`), blockers and findings, closure (`close_release`), embarked-ticket labelling;
- the release pass in `release_advance.py`: candidate in flight, gate reading, release-please PR merge(s), stagnation, deployed ledger, finished-cycle closure;
- `release_fixes.py`, `release_contracts.py`, `release_ticket_notes.py`, the `release` verbs of `linear_cli.py`, `release_audio.release_status` semantics, `scripts/verify-release-notes.py`, `scripts/gate_auto_rerun.py`, `.github/release-gate/known-flakes.json`.

It **does not** specify the Python QA engine (`release_qa*.py`, model fan-out, consolidation prompts), the MCP/audio facade, the fleet/heartbeat, 2Do, Telegram transport, `sudo`/box paths, or the vault tables. Those are listed in "Dropped" with reasons. Where the target design differs from the Python (QA by agents on angle branches, issue-after-tag ordering, bridge-posted brief for every product, bridge-triggered closure, notifier-sent release note, human requalification), the requirement is written for the **target** and the Python behaviour it replaces is named in "Notes".

### 1.1 Conventions

- **Section numbers in cross-references:** `§3` = *Dropped from the Python (with reason)* (its sub-sections 3.1-3.4); `§4` = *Test cases to carry over*; `§5` = *Open questions* (numbered 1-20).
- **MUST / SHOULD / MAY** are used in the RFC 2119 sense. A statement in bold is the requirement; "Source" cites Python at `origin/main` as `file:line` (repo-relative; `tests/…` for pinning tests); "Notes" give edge cases.
- **Fail closed.** Every read that fails, is malformed, or is ambiguous yields "not covered / not green / not judged / not merged". "I could not tell" is never "nothing there". This is the dominant rule of the chain; most REQs restate it for their own reads.
- **Tick.** One bridge poll cycle. The bridge MUST be safe to run at every tick and idempotent: running it twice on the same facts writes nothing the second time (REQ-REL-100).
- **One irreversible action per repository per tick** (REQ-REL-071): a release-please PR merge, a gate-record PR merge, an angle-PR merge, or the closure. Everything else is a read or a comment.
- **Language.** Every string written to the tracker, to GitHub (PR titles/bodies, comments) or to a notifier is **English**. The French strings of the Python are not carried (the reason sentences are re-stated in English in REQ-REL-067). Machine-read markers are ASCII and language-neutral.
- **Release item** = the tracker item (Linear issue / Livela item) that represents one release of one product version. **Angle** = one QA review axis (e.g. security, design, product) configured per repo. **Candidate / RC** = annotated tag `<product>-v<X.Y.Z>-rc.<N>`. **Cycle** = one `release/<stamp>-utc` branch from `--open` to merge-back. **Embarked ticket** = a tracker ticket linked to a PR that merged a commit of the candidate range (REQ-REL-042).
- **Logical ticket states** the `Tracker` trait must expose (names are the backend's concern): `Triage`, `Backlog`, `Ready`, `InProgress`, `InReview`, `ToDeploy` (merged, not yet released; a "completed" column), `PostDeploy`, `Done`, `Canceled`; plus the **state kind** of each (`triage|backlog|unstarted|started|completed|canceled`). A state whose kind is unknown counts as **open** everywhere (`release_service.py:1716-1724`, `1784-1786`).

### 1.2 Target lifecycle in one paragraph

A person (or the bridge, REQ-REL-065) dispatches the cut workflow (`open` or `candidate`). The workflow creates `release/<stamp>-utc` from `main` (open only), waits for release-please's run on it, reads the staged version from release-please's PR, and pushes an annotated `…-rc.N` tag whose annotation carries `Release-Class`. The tag push starts the CI gate workflow, which only **publishes GitHub facts** (run conclusion on the tag SHA, `suites.json` evidence artifact, class resolved from the annotation). At its next tick the bridge sees an RC tag with no release item and **creates the release item** (issue follows the tag; nothing else starts without it), records the candidate and labels the embarked tickets. Once the gate of the latest RC is green, and never before (BR-ASDLC-004), the bridge creates one **sub-item per angle** and the consolidation item, then posts the **brief** (from GitHub facts, every product, linking the gate's evidence pack); an agent opens `qa/<candidate>/<angle>` from the release branch, commits blocking fixes plus a mandatory **report file**, and opens a PR to the release branch naming its sub-item. The coordinator reviews and approves; the bridge merges the angle PR on approval and files tickets for non-blocking findings from the merged report. When the release branch has moved, a new RC is cut, up to `max_rc_passes` (default 2), with full QA on each. The **GO** is computed (REQ-REL-066): gate green on the latest RC + all angle PRs merged + zero open blocker + release-please note covers the range + fixes in candidate + RC equals branch tip. On GO the bridge squash-merges the release-please PR(s) (single or multi-component, record first, version component last). CI publishes and merges the release branch back; the bridge sees the release published, the merge-back merged (and, multi-component, every embarked component labelled `deployed: production`), **judges every embarked ticket** (Done / Post Deploy / back to In Progress), verifies cited business-rule ids against the registry **at the release tag**, closes the release item, and **sends the release note** through the notifier.

## Requirements

### 2.1 Cut (dispatch) and branch/tag rules

**REQ-REL-001 — The cut is one `workflow_dispatch` with two gestures: `open` and `candidate`.** The workflow MUST take an `action` input (`open` | `candidate`, default `open`), an optional `class` input (`routine` | `sensible` | empty) and an optional `dry_run` boolean. `open` starts a cycle (creates `release/<stamp>-utc` from `main`, then `rc.1`); `candidate` puts `rc.N+1` on the release branch already in flight. Opening a cycle and tagging a new candidate are different acts with different preconditions and different starting refs and MUST NOT be one button. Unknown `action` or unknown `class` MUST fail before anything touches git or the network, naming the accepted values; a missing value after `--class` MUST be a named error (never a bare exit).
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:38-48,82-135`; `.github/workflows/release.yml` (inputs `action`, `class`; preflight step); `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:3797` (both gestures), `:1371` (unknown class), `:1383` (missing value).
Notes: The bridge itself MAY dispatch `action=candidate` (REQ-REL-065); a human dispatches `open`. No `AGENTFORGE_RUNNER_READY` preflight, no `sudo`, no instance identity (dropped, see §3).

**REQ-REL-002 — A candidate is only ever cut from a branch that equals its remote.** `open` MUST run on `main` and `main`'s tip MUST equal `origin/main` (stale `main` refused). `candidate` MUST run on a branch matching the release-branch pattern (REQ-REL-004) that exists on `origin` and whose tip equals `origin/<branch>`; on `main`, a feature branch or any other ref it MUST refuse and say what to do instead (`open` a cycle). A tag on a commit `origin` has never seen certifies code that exists for nobody else. Tracked-file modifications in the working tree MUST refuse the cut; untracked files are ignored (a tag captures committed content only).
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:137-199`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:1476` (refuse from main), `:1495` (branch not its remote), `:2004` (fresh annotated ref, never moves one).
Notes: In a workflow the checkout is the remote tip by construction; the requirement survives as "the tag's target commit is the current tip of the release branch on origin, read via the API at tag time". The local-vs-remote comparison is dropped as a local-clone check (§3).

**REQ-REL-003 — One cycle in flight per repository.** `open` MUST refuse while any `refs/heads/release/*` exists on `origin`, naming every such branch and saying that finishing that cycle (or abandoning it by hand: carry merged PRs to `main`, delete the branch, cancel the release item) is the only way out. The exit status of the listing MUST be kept separate from its content: an unreachable `origin` (dropped network, expired token, paused App) MUST refuse the cut ("could not list the release branches"), never read as "no branch in flight".
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:223-258`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:1630`, `:1649` (second cycle the same day), `:1915` (unreadable listing refuses).
Notes: Abandoning a cycle is the one deliberately manual step of this model; no code removes a leftover branch. Two release branches mean two release-please PRs and two tags for one version, which the model cannot survive.

**REQ-REL-004 — Release branch name is `release/<YYYY-MM-DD-HH-MM>-utc`.** The name MUST be built from the **UTC** wall clock, to the **minute**, with the literal `-utc` suffix, and MUST be validated by `^release/[0-9]{4}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}-[0-9]{2}-utc$` wherever a candidate is cut or a branch is recognised. The retired `release/<YYYY-MM-DD>` shape is refused. A leftover local branch of the same stamp from an interrupted attempt MUST NOT fail the cut (branch is force-created from the current tip).
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:145-153,260-273`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:4014` (leftover local branch), `:1517`.
Notes: UTC (not local time) so the name and the GitHub run history tell the same day, and no repeated minute at a DST fold. The name does not carry the version: the version is only known once release-please has run on the branch.

**REQ-REL-005 — The branch is created from the tip of `main` and pushed with a credential that triggers workflows; no extra dispatch.** `open` MUST create the branch from the current tip of `main`, push it, and let the **push** be release-please's trigger (`release-please.yml` runs on `push: branches: ['release/**']` with `target-branch: ${{ github.ref_name }}`). The push MUST be authenticated as a GitHub App installation (events created with `GITHUB_TOKEN` start no workflow). The cut MUST NOT dispatch `release-please.yml` after the push: two triggers make two runs and "which run answers this push" stops being answerable. `rc.1` MUST point at the tip of the new branch.
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:277-307`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:1582` (never dispatches), `:1540` (branch exists on origin; rc.1 on its tip), `:3241-3262` (release-please runs on `release/**` only, targets the branch it runs on, refuses dispatch on other refs).
Notes: `workflow_dispatch` stays declared on `release-please.yml` for one legitimate use, the bridge's single redispatch of a stale PR (REQ-REL-075).

**REQ-REL-006 — Waiting for release-please is event-driven, never a clock.** After the push, `open` MUST read the **most recent** `release-please.yml` run on that branch (`--limit 1`: the branch name is unique per cycle, so that run is this push's answer) and decide:
- none registered yet, or `status != completed` → keep waiting, however long; a zero-row listing is the ordinary "not registered yet";
- completed and `conclusion != success` → refuse now, naming the run URL;
- completed, `success`, but no release-please PR → refuse now (nothing staged to release), naming the run URL;
- the listing call itself failing (expired token, 5xx, workflow absent) → refuse now with the call's own error — it is an event, not a wait.
The poll interval is configurable (default 15 s) and only spaces polls; the only upper bound is the job's own timeout (20 min). `gh run list` `conclusion` is `""` for a run in progress: the filter MUST map both `null` and `""` to a placeholder so a tab-separated row never collapses a column.
On every refusal after the push, **the pushed branch MUST be left in place** (it holds the run history and is what makes the next `open` refuse instead of opening a second cycle) and the message MUST give the resume command and the abandon command.
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:341-483,389-464`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:1675` (never opened its PR), `:1727` (waits on the event not a clock), `:1760` (failed run → refuse now), `:1790` (list failure → refuse now), `:1829` (refusal names the run URL), `:1852` (looks up by workflow + branch), `:1878` (keeps waiting while in progress), `:4302` (empty conclusion mapped to a dash).
Notes: Replaces a flat 300 s wait that refused a healthy 6-minute run. The PR lookup (REQ-REL-007) happens only after a `success`.

**REQ-REL-007 — The RC version is read from release-please's PR body, fail-closed.** The release PR MUST be found by **base branch + label `autorelease: pending`** (never by head-branch name); if no labelled PR, by base + head-branch **prefix** `release-please--`. The version is the first match of `<summary>\s*<product>\s*:\s*(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)` in the body (the PR title is the fixed `chore: release …` and carries none). Rules:
- `open`: no PR after a successful run → **refuse** (never fall back to the manifest/`pyproject` version, which is the version already published and would start the gate and a release item on a finished cycle);
- `candidate`: the PR is read fresh at that instant (no run history, no polling). If none is open, the Python falls back to the manifest version with a warning.
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:310-339,485-576`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:1610` (looked up by base and label), `:1675`.
Notes: **Changed from Python (flagged in §5):** the target SHOULD replace the manifest fallback on the `candidate` path by "reuse the version of the newest existing RC of this cycle's branch, else refuse", because the manifest holds the last *released* version, not the pending one. A body with two products' summary lines: the one whose product equals the configured product wins (REQ-REL-073). The shell parser is stricter than the pass's (`<summary>agentforge: X.Y.Z` literal, one space, hard-coded product, POSIX/BSD `sed` for macOS checkouts); the Rust implementation MUST use **one** lenient parser (`<summary>\s*<product>\s*:\s*X.Y.Z`) for the cut and for the staged-version guard, since both read the same contract of the action.

**REQ-REL-008 — RC numbering and tag immutability.** The next RC number MUST be the lowest `N ≥ 1` such that `<product>-v<version>-rc.<N>` does not exist (after fetching tags). The tag MUST be created as a **new annotated ref and pushed without force**; an existing tag name MUST never be moved or recreated. A tag cannot drift, so "this SHA passed the gate" stays true.
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:587-601,873`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:2004` (SR-006), `:1960` (rc.N+1 goes on the branch's new tip, `main` not consulted), `:1300` (second round names the previous candidate).
Notes: The Python prints a note when round > 2 ("the earlier project capped this at two rounds"); the target turns that into `max_rc_passes` enforcement (REQ-REL-063/064).

**REQ-REL-009 — The RC tag is annotated and carries the class in its annotation.** The tag MUST be an annotated tag (message: `Release candidate <tag>`) whose annotation body contains, each on its own line, `Release-Class: <routine|sensible>` and `Release-Class-Reason: <text>`. The reason MUST be a separate line, never appended to the class line (the reader anchors on end-of-line; anything appended makes the candidate read as unclassified). The class travels in the tag and nowhere else (not a workflow input, not a repository variable): the tag is immutable, so is its classification.
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:15-24,815-826`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:1412` (end-to-end: class written is the class the gate reads), `:569` (resolver reads the annotation), `:2428`.
Notes: A lightweight tag has no message to carry a class; it is "unclassified" (REQ-REL-010).

**REQ-REL-010 — Reading the class of a candidate.** Anything that needs a candidate's class (the gate workflow, the bridge for the brief and the candidate comment) MUST read it from the tag annotation only, and only from a **real tag object** (`%(objecttype) = tag`): `%(contents)` of a lightweight tag is the commit message and a `Release-Class:` line in a commit body is not a classification. Accepted values are exactly `routine` and `sensible`; anything else, an absent line, or a lightweight tag MUST read as `unclassified` and MUST NEVER be promoted to `routine`. An explicit `workflow_dispatch` input outranks the tag. The checkout that reads it MUST fetch tag objects. The resolved class MUST be recorded in the evidence pack (`suites.json`/QA report).
Source: `.github/workflows/pre-release-qa.yml:177-230`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:562-660` (resolved class exposed; dispatch honoured; lightweight rejected; unclassified not quietly routine; checkout fetches the tag object; class recorded in the evidence pack; brief reads the resolved class, never `|| 'routine'`), `:2115`.
Notes: Fixes a past incident: `github.event.inputs` is null on a tag push, so a dispatch-only read classified every real release `routine`. The two **written** values are exactly `routine` and `sensible`; `unclassified` is a value only the **reader** emits, and every consumer (brief, candidate comment, any wrapper) MUST accept it — refusing it would turn "nobody classified this candidate" into "the brief never posts" (`tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:2389`, `:3946`).

#### Release class (routine / sensible)

**REQ-REL-011 — The class is computed from the diff against the repository's list of sensitive paths; failures classify `sensible`.** `classify(changed_paths, repo_table)` MUST return `(class, reason)`:
- `changed_paths` absent/unreadable/empty (no previous release tag, `git diff` failed, empty stdin) → `sensible`, reason "the diff since the last release could not be read" — an empty list means "nobody could tell me", never "nothing changed";
- `repo_table` = the text of the repository's **own** sensitive-path list (`.github/review-paths.json`) **as the candidate carries it** (read at `HEAD`/the candidate ref, handed over as text): parsed as JSON object with a **non-empty** `paths` list of non-empty strings; unparseable, empty or malformed → `sensible` (reason "the review table could not be read…"), **never** a fallback to another list;
- no repository list at the candidate (file absent) → the fallback table `repos.<repo>.paths` of the configured review table applies; no such table/repo/entry → `sensible`;
- any changed path matching a sensitive glob (REQ-REL-012) → `sensible`, reason `touches the reviewed path <first hit>`; otherwise `routine`, reason `no reviewed path touched`.
The class is a fact about the diff, not a judgement taken at a prompt. The diff is `<last final tag>..HEAD` (`git diff --name-only`), `LAST_RELEASE` being the highest non-RC `<product>-v*` tag by version.
Source: `release_service.py:1520-1603`; `scripts/cut-rc.sh:607-626,724-772`; `linear_cli.py:253-284`; `pr_merge.py:335-352` (list parse); `tests/test_release_service.py:4582-4701` (ci diff sensible; docs-only routine; unreadable/absent/unknown table sensible; unreadable diff sensible; directory glob; list read at candidate ref decides; repo list replaces vault's; unreadable repo list sensible and never falls back; no repo list falls back), `tests/test_linear_cli.py:156-222`, `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:2428-2550`.
Notes: Fail-closed direction is deliberate: an over-classified candidate costs a reviewer one checklist item; an under-classified one silently removes a reviewer step. In the target the list is `[release].review_paths` in `spl-asdlc.toml` **or** the repo's `.github/review-paths.json` read at the candidate ref (the latter wins, as in Python); the vault table fallback is dropped (§3). The verb prints one TAB-separated line `<class>\t<reason>`.

**REQ-REL-012 — Sensitive-path glob semantics (shared by class, angle scope, brief).** `sensitive_hits(paths, patterns)` MUST return, deduplicated in input order, each path matching any pattern where "matches" means: shell-style `fnmatch(path, pattern)` (where `*` also crosses `/`, `?`, `[seq]`) **or** the pattern with trailing `*` and `/` stripped is a non-empty directory prefix and the path starts with `<prefix>/`. Consequences that tests pin: `a/**` matches `a/b/c`; the literal pattern `**` matches every path; `docs/**` does not match an unrelated top-level file; a path nested under a directory glob matches.
Source: `pr_merge.py:1564-1581`; `tests/test_release_service.py:590-612` (empty scope and `**` mean every candidate; directory glob does not match an unrelated top-level file / matches nested), `:4642`.
Notes: Rust's `glob`/`globset` semantics differ from Python's `fnmatch` (`*` crossing `/`); implement the Python rule explicitly or the class and scope decisions drift from the verified behaviour.

**REQ-REL-013 — A forced class wins unconditionally.** `--class`/`class=` input, when given, MUST be used as the class without querying the table, with reason `forced with --class`; an invalid value MUST be refused before anything else (REQ-REL-001). A cut MUST never refuse for lack of a class and MUST never default an unknown class to `routine`; if no answer can be computed (no interpreter, unreadable list, empty diff) the class is `sensible` with reason "the candidate class could not be computed".
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:127-135,742-772`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:2531` (explicit class wins), `:2550` (no longer refuses without a class), `:1396`, `:2510` (falls back to sensible).

**REQ-REL-014 — Diff bases.** Two bases MUST be computed and kept apart: (a) the diff since the **last published release** (`LAST_RELEASE`; for class, brief and "tickets included") and (b) the diff since the **previous candidate** of the same version (`<product>-v<version>-rc.<N-1>`, for re-review scope), falling back to `LAST_RELEASE` for rc.1. On rc.N>1 a missing `rc.N-1` tag MUST be reported as "previous candidate unresolvable", distinct from "first candidate". When no release was ever published for the line, the cumulative diff falls back to the earliest known candidate and the brief MUST say so (`previous_tag_is_release=false`), never claim a release that does not exist.
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:828-840`; `.github/workflows/pre-release-qa.yml:702-799`; `release_service.py:1377-1489`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:414-496,1300`, `tests/test_release_service.py:1610-1696`.

**REQ-REL-015 — Dry run.** With `dry_run`, the cut MUST describe the branch it would create, the round (`rc.N`), the commit and the class, and MUST leave the repository and `origin` untouched (no branch, no tag, no push). Its version is the manifest's (release-please cannot stage one before the branch exists), so only the branch name and round number are contractual.
Source: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:264-308,568-576,803-813`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:1453`, `:1517`.

**REQ-REL-016 — The cut writes nothing to the tracker; the issue follows the tag.** (Reversal of Python.) The cut workflow MUST NOT require a tracker identity, MUST NOT run a preflight against the tracker, and MUST NOT create or unwind a tag around tracker writes. The release item is created by the **bridge at its next tick** after it sees the RC tag (REQ-REL-039). Consequently **nothing downstream starts without the item**: no angle sub-item, no brief, no GO, no merge is produced for a candidate whose release item does not exist; the bridge creates it first and reports a candidate without an item that it cannot create.
Source (Python, to be reversed): `scripts/cut-rc.sh:629-643,774-801,854-873` (preflight → local tag → `release open` → push; local tag deleted if the issue fails); `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:1175` (refuse without identity), `:1203` (unwinds tag when the issue fails), `:1226` (creates issue then pushes), `:1248`, `:1278`.
Notes: The GitHub tag is irreversible once pushed (no unwinding); that is why issue creation MUST be idempotent and repairable at any later tick, and why fail-closed moves from "refuse to tag" to "refuse to advance".

**REQ-REL-017 — Fixes during a cycle target the release branch, never `main`; CI contracts the chain relies on.** A fix PR for a candidate in flight has the release branch as base. The chain relies on these repository/CI facts (specified so the CI side can be built to match; they are not bridge writes):
- `release-please` does not run on `main`; it runs on `push` to `release/**` with `target-branch` = the ref;
- `pr-check`/post-merge workflows run on `release/**` so a hotfix PR reports the required check; the `release/**` ruleset requires it;
- the merge-back PR is `release/<stamp>-utc` → `main`, opened as the same App (so it triggers workflows), merged as a **merge commit — never a squash** (the release tag must become an ancestor of `main`), and the branch is deleted **only after** the merge landed or the tag is already an ancestor of `main`;
- `main` is **never** merged into the release branch (no `update-branch`, no sync PR, no conflict resolved on the merge-back PR); a real `DIRTY` conflict is resolved on a dedicated `chore/merge-back-<stamp>` branch off the release branch's tip and landed on `main` as a merge commit;
- a failed merge-back leaves the PR open, files a **blocking** item against the release and does not close the release; replaying the close (`close_tag`) republishes nothing;
- there is **no freeze window**: nothing holds `main` shut during a cycle (the freeze workflows, `release-window-status.sh` and the `freeze` table key are deleted, not disabled — `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:3986`);
- the bridge MUST NOT squash-merge, nor itself merge, a PR whose head is `release/**` or the record branch prefix, and an unreadable head name is treated as a merge-back (never an ordinary PR).
Source: `docs/RELEASE_PROCESS.md` §"Branche de release courte" items 1-10; `.github/workflows/release-publish.yml:795-1086`; `pr_merge.py:1488-1520` (`is_merge_back`); `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:3241-3430,3437-3770` (merge-back before close, merge commit never squash, deletes branch only after merge, failed merge-back files a blocking issue, poll handles BEHIND/BLOCKED/DIRTY, no `update-branch` anywhere, close-tag replay publishes nothing, merge-back PR opened as the App), `:2013` (SR-005).
Notes: The bridge's role here is read-only (REQ-REL-082) plus never merging those PRs. The recovery instructions text is CI/docs.

### 2.2 Candidate range and release-please note coverage

**REQ-REL-018 — The candidate range is Git's, from the product's last final tag strictly below the candidate.** `candidate_commits(product, candidate, since?)` MUST return `(base, commits)` where `commits` = `git log --reverse <base>..<candidate>` as `(sha, subject)`, oldest first, and `base` = `since` if given else the product's **highest FINAL tag strictly below the candidate's version**. Final tags are `git tag --list "<prefix>*" --sort=-v:refname` entries not containing `-rc.`; "strictly below" means the comparison is on the parsed `(major,minor,patch)`, so re-running on a candidate whose own final tag is already published still reads the cycle it belongs to; a candidate whose version cannot be parsed (a branch name before the cut) falls back to the highest final tag. If no such tag exists the call MUST refuse ("no published `<prefix>*` tag below `<candidate>`: refusing to read the whole history"). The tag prefix is the **published-tag** shape (`<product>-v` by default, `release_shape.release_tag_prefix` for a product that publishes a component tag, e.g. `app-v`). There is exactly **one** definition of this range: the brief, the embarked-ticket list, the note guard at GO, the publication backstop and the closure preview MUST all call it (none lists tags itself).
Source: `release_range.py:36-39,87-153`; `tests/test_release_range.py:22` (keeps commits older than the previous tag — the 0.4.118 shape), `:36`, `:48` (component tag prefix), `:71`, `:87` (default prefix unchanged), `:97` (no published tag refuses), `:110` (embarked tickets read the same range), `:128` (one definition of the previous tag).
Notes: The range is Git's, not release-please's: release-please walks commit **dates**, and the short-lived branch merges its tagged predecessor back into a moving `main`, so a commit made on `main` during the previous cycle is dated before that tag yet is part of the next candidate. Parsing tags: `^(?P<product>[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*)-v(?P<version>\d+\.\d+\.\d+)(?:-rc\.(?P<rc>\d+))?$`; the product is whatever precedes the **last** `-v<semver>` (`twodo-reminders-sync-v1.3.1` has hyphens of its own). Git calls MUST never raise: a git that cannot run is a failed read (non-zero), with a 120 s timeout.

**REQ-REL-019 — The candidate must resolve to a commit; `contains(commit, candidate)` is tri-state.** `candidate_commits` MUST refuse a candidate that does not resolve (`rev-parse --verify --quiet <c>^{commit}`), by name. `contains(commit, candidate)` answers "is `commit` the candidate or one of its ancestors" via `merge-base --is-ancestor`: exit 0 → true, exit 1 → false, **anything else raises** (a commit this clone does not have — late fetch, PR of another repo — or a candidate that is not a ref): "unknown" is not "not contained" and the caller reports it as "could not tell". Only a full 40-hex commit id may reach git.
Source: `release_range.py:56,125-181`; `tests/test_release_range.py:105,139-167`.

**REQ-REL-020 — Release-note ownership rule: every Conventional commit of the range is named in the note.** A commit is **owed a line** iff its subject matches `^(feat|fix|perf|revert|docs|style|chore|refactor|test|build|ci)(\([^)]+\))?!?:\s+(.+)$` (and its type is written by the config, REQ-REL-021). If the subject ends in `(#N)` the note MUST contain the **complete numeric token** `#N` (`(?<!\d)#N(?!\d)`: `#7830` is not evidence for PR #783, `#750` not for #75); otherwise the commit's summary text MUST appear as a substring of the note. A subject that is not Conventional is not owed a line. `missing_entries` returns the owed-but-absent commits in range order.
Source: `release_notes.py:36-41,105-131`; `tests/test_release_notes.py:23-77,155` (complete note covers; the 0.4.118 note omitting five commits names all five; one omission suffices; longer PR number is not coverage; direct commit covered by its summary; non-conventional not owed; hidden type not missing but visible is), `tests/test_verify_release_notes.py:31,92,135,155`.

**REQ-REL-021 — Which commit types are owed is read from the repo's release-please config; unknowns relax nothing.** `written_types(config_text)`: parse JSON; take `packages["."]` (must be an object, else *cannot tell*); `changelog-sections` from the root package, else top-level; absent → the always-written set `{feat, fix, perf, revert}`; not a list → *cannot tell*; a list → the set of `type`s of entries that are not `hidden`. **None (cannot tell: no config, bad JSON, wrong shape) relaxes nothing: every Conventional type is owed.** Only an explicit config can lower the bar. The config is read **at the same commit** as the note.
Source: `release_notes.py:59-92`; `tests/test_release_notes.py:124-155` (hidden types not owed; config without sections owes the four always-written; unreadable config relaxes nothing; this repo's config writes all eleven), `tests/test_release_advance.py:979,1001` (hidden type not held against the note; visible type still owed), `tests/test_verify_release_notes.py:192`.

**REQ-REL-022 — Note section and coverage result; unreadable is a gap, not coverage.** `release_section(version, changelog)` MUST return the text from the `## [<version>]` heading line up to (exclusive) the next `## [` heading, and refuse when no such heading exists. `coverage(candidate, changelog, types)` MUST return `NoteCoverage{candidate, previous, missing[], unreadable}`: the candidate must parse as `<product>-vX.Y.Z[-rc.N]` (else unreadable); a missing section, an empty changelog, or a range that cannot be read (REQ-REL-018/019) yields `unreadable=<reason>`; `covered` ⇔ no missing and not unreadable. A PR named only in the **previous** version's section is not covered by this one. `gaps` renders one line per missing commit `"<sha12> <subject>"`, or the single line "note unreadable: <reason>" when unreadable.
Source: `release_notes.py:95-102,134-179`; `tests/test_release_notes.py:84,95,99,108` (no section/empty changelog/unreadable range are unreadable not covered; the section stops at the next version).

**REQ-REL-023 — The note is judged at the release-please PR's head commit — the one the squash will take — never at a branch name.** The coverage read MUST use the PR's `head_sha` (a full 40-hex id; otherwise `unreadable`: "the PR does not expose a readable head commit"), reading `CHANGELOG.md` and `release-please-config.json` **at that commit**; a file unreadable at that commit is an unreadable note (and an unreadable config merely relaxes nothing). The merge MUST be pinned to the judged head (REQ-REL-072): release-please force-pushes its branch, so a head that moved since the read is refused by GitHub and re-judged at the next tick; a commit pushed onto the PR's branch that completes the note is picked up at the next tick. No PR open yet is **not** a gap (nothing to merge, nothing to judge; the redispatch logic REQ-REL-075 owns that case); a PR list that could not be read is a gap ("note not verifiable: the release-please PR could not be read"); a candidate whose release branch could not be read is a gap; a candidate on no release branch yields no gap (the cycle is over: the publication backstop judges the merged file).
Source: `release_advance.py:1004-1075,1098-1121`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:875-1132` (omitting note stops the merge and names all five; covering note merges; read at the squash commit; merge pinned to the judged commit; completed note merges next tick; incomparable note never merged; unreadable head commit never merged; range read from previous final tag; no PR is not a gap; stuck release names commits in the warning; gap beside open review still named; behind PR still redispatched when note incomplete; readers told the gaps; reader with no checkout says so; reader resolves checkout under the pass identity), `tests/test_release_audio.py:330-392`, `tests/test_linear_cli.py:1048-1109`.
Notes: Multi-component shapes (REQ-REL-076) have per-component CHANGELOGs under `apps/<x>/`; the single-note coverage is **not** applied to them (declared property of the shape, not a failed read).

**REQ-REL-024 — Completion lines are suggested, never written.** For commits a note omits, `suggested_entries(missing, repo_url)` MUST produce release-please-format lines: `* **<scope>:** <text> ([#N](<repo_url>/issues/N))`, or `* <text> (#N)` with no `repo_url`, or `* <text>` for a commit with neither scope nor PR; the `(#N)` suffix is stripped from the text before reformatting. They are for a person to paste into the release-please PR; **nothing writes a replacement note on anyone's behalf**. The bridge MAY include them in the HOLD/stagnation comment.
Source: `release_notes.py:182-201`; `tests/test_release_notes.py:167,186`.

**REQ-REL-025 — A post-merge backstop verifies the merged note; same function, same range.** A CI step at publication MUST run the note coverage on the **merged** `CHANGELOG.md` against the candidate (the newest `<release-tag>-rc.*` that is an ancestor of the published commit), exit non-zero naming the omitted commits `<sha12> <subject>` and the suggested lines, and succeed with a message otherwise. It MUST refuse when the release tag and candidate tag do not parse or are not of the same `(product, version)`, when `CHANGELOG.md` is unreadable, and when coverage is unreadable. In the target this is a subcommand (`spl-asdlc release verify-note --release-tag … --candidate-tag … --repo …`) of the same crate (no Python on the runner), and MUST depend on nothing beyond git and the standard library.
Source: `scripts/verify-release-notes.py:48-95`; `release_range.py:1-26` (std-lib-only rationale); `tests/test_verify_release_notes.py:31-192` (finds an older main commit; longer PR number not coverage; the 0.4.118 note refused naming five; completed note passes; candidate of another version not judged; runs without the package installed; owes only the types the config writes), `tests/test_release_notes.py:193` (guard needs nothing beyond the standard library).
Notes: It should never refuse once the GO's note condition (REQ-REL-066) has held, but remains for the day the pass could not read the PR. It never writes a note.

**REQ-REL-026 — Release bookkeeping is recognised from facts, not titles.** A merged PR is **bookkeeping** (never a change a note describes, never embarks a ticket) iff its head ref starts with `release-please--` or `release/` — a fact GitHub reports, not text anyone wrote.
Source: `release_contracts.py:131-151`; `tests/test_release_ticket_notes.py:115`.

### 2.3 Release shape per product (tags, components, record)

**REQ-REL-027 — A product's release shape is data in `spl-asdlc.toml`, with an AgentForge-shaped default.** The shape MUST carry: `repo`, `candidate_tag` template (must contain `{version}` and `{rc}` exactly once each), `release_tag` template (`{version}` exactly once, no `{rc}`), `components[]` (empty = default single-PR shape), `version_component` (the component whose staged version must be the candidate's; must be one of `components`; forbidden without components), `release_please_authors[]` (**required** when components exist), `record` template (`{version}` once, no `{rc}`; **required** when components exist, because every component's deploy refuses without the gate record). Default shape: candidate `<product>-v{version}-rc.{rc}`, release tag `<product>-v{version}`, no components, no record. Derived: `multi_component = bool(components)`; `candidate_glob` = template with `{version}`/`{rc}` → `*`; `candidate_re` = the escaped template with `(?P<version>\d+\.\d+\.\d+)` and `(?P<rc>\d+)`, anchored; `parse(tag) → (X.Y.Z, rc) | None`; `release_tag(version)` = template formatted with `version.lstrip("v")`; `release_tag_prefix` = literal text before `{version}` (`product-v`, `app-v`); `record_path(version)` = formatted template or "".
Source: `release_shape.py:1-37,47-130`; `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:740` (parses candidates and names the closing tag), `:767` (release-tag prefix is the template before `{version}`), `:228` (unprefixed candidate read through the declared shape).
Notes: a multi-component project example: candidate `v{version}-rc.{rc}` (no product prefix), release tag `app-v{version}`, components `app/-website/-admin`, version component `app`, record `docs/releases/v{version}.md`. Python's per-checkout product matching (`declared_shape` by directory name; "two products claim one checkout" refusal) disappears: one `spl-asdlc.toml` = one repo = one shape (REQ-REL-101).

**REQ-REL-028 — A declared shape that cannot be read is a refusal, never the default.** `parse_shape` MUST raise `ShapeError` (a named reason) on: non-object entry; missing `repo`; bad candidate/release templates; `components` not a list of non-empty strings; `version_component` not among components, or set without components; components without authors; components without record; bad record template. The regex MUST be compiled at parse time so a bad template fails at load, not mid-tick. A tick whose shape is unreadable MUST **skip** that repo with the reason (guessing a product's shape is how a tag of the wrong component gets called "the candidate").
Source: `release_shape.py:133-190,193-225,228-246`; `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:260,762,789,799,804,810`, `tests/test_release_advance.py:1975` (unreadable release-please config fails closed).

**REQ-REL-029 — Product identity is configured, and cross-checked against release-please's root package when one exists.** The bridge MUST know the product name from config. Where the repo has `release-please-config.json`: an unreadable file → refuse with a reason; a config with no root package (`packages["."]`) → refuse ("declares no root package, so no product can be named"); `package-name` else `component` of the root package names the product (a root package naming none → refuse). Candidate tags of **other** products in the same repo (`twodo-reminders-sync-v*-rc.*`) MUST be ignored and logged, never taken as the candidate in flight.
Source: `release_advance.py:227-268,340-368`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:1923,1957,1975,1988,1999` (another product's candidate is never this repo's; own product still advanced beside another; unreadable config fails closed; a second product never masks the first).
Notes: A repo with no release-please config at all has no restriction (no second product can fight over its release item).

**REQ-REL-030 — Identifying the release-please PR: three signals to find it, authorship to trust it.** A PR is **release-please-shaped** iff it carries label `autorelease: pending` **or** a head branch with prefix `release-please--` **or** a title matching `^chore(\(.*\))?:\s*release\b` (case-insensitive). It is **the** release PR iff, additionally, it is open against the cycle's release branch (`base == branch`, never a repository-wide search), and its author login is one of the configured `release_please_authors` (both spellings of an App login are accepted: `<slug>[bot]` and `app/<slug>`) **and** `author_is_bot` is not `False`. A shape-matching PR with another author (a human whose login merely resembles the App, a different bot, a former release App) MUST be logged and ignored exactly like "no PR": shape is not proof of origin, and this is the one place a shape-match turns into an unreviewed squash-merge. The lookup also reports `shape_seen` (any PR against the base had the shape, even one rejected on authorship) so that "release-please never opened a PR" can be told from "a PR occupies the shape under the wrong author". `base` is REQUIRED: an empty base MUST be refused, not read as "search the whole repo" (a wide search finds a stale `chore: release main` PR and merging it publishes off the integration branch).
Source: `release_advance.py:122-141,783-952`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:585-681` (other author never merged; the `app/…` login form accepted; service identity merged; raw `[bot]` accepted; former release App refused; human lookalike refused (`is_bot: False`); unrelated bot refused), `:1810` (found by base not head name), `:1823` (stale PR against main never merged), `:1887` (never looked for without a base), `:1988` (label alone identifies).
Notes: `list_open_prs` folds a failed `gh pr list` into `[]`; the release pass MUST distinguish "answered: nothing open" from "could not answer" (`read_ok`), because redispatch acts on the former only. List at most 30 open PRs. (Authors constant in Python is `<service-app>`; the target takes it from `[release].release_please_authors`, no default.)

**REQ-REL-031 — The candidate in flight is the newest candidate of the product whose release is not yet published, read fail-closed.** For the default shape: list `*-v*-rc.*` tags (`--sort=-v:refname`), keep the newest per product, restrict to the repo's product (REQ-REL-029); for a declared shape: the newest tag matching `candidate_glob` **and** `parse()` (older unpublished candidates of abandoned cycles are not the one in flight). Then, in order: tag listing failed → "the candidate tags could not be listed"; no candidate of its own → quiet "nothing to advance" (also when the fetch failed: nothing to be wrong about); fetch of tags (`--tags --prune`, never prompting for credentials) failed on a repo that has candidates → "the tags could not be refreshed, so no candidate can be read off them"; `release view <release_tag>` says not found → candidate is in flight; found → none in flight (the cycle is over for this pass); any other failure → "whether `<tag>` is published could not be read". Re-cutting `rc.N+1` of the same version moves which tag answers and nothing else.
Source: `release_advance.py:271-477,415-438`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:725-798,1782,1923,2042-2078` (stale tags stop the pass; unlistable tag set stops it; an unreadable release is not read as unpublished; a published release closes the question; no candidate is not an error; repo that never cut a candidate is skipped quietly; stale tag list on a repo that does release fails closed; quiet even when fetch fails with no candidate history), `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:228-260`.
Notes: In the target "tags" are read via the GitHub API or a clone; the fail-closed cases are unchanged: a failed tag read is "cannot tell", never "none".

**REQ-REL-032 — "Release published?" separates "does not exist" from "could not read".** The check is the GitHub release of `shape.release_tag(version)`: success → published; failure whose message matches `release not found|HTTP 404` (case-insensitive) → not published (an answer); **any other failure** (including `could not find any git remote pointing to a GitHub host`) → unreadable, reported as `(gh exit N)` and treated as "cannot tell". Matching a bare "could not find" is forbidden: it would make a shipped release look like a candidate still in flight and re-merge a publication.
Source: `release_advance.py:480-511`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:747,760,772,2078`.

**REQ-REL-033 — The release branch of a candidate is read off the refs, with three answers.** `candidate_branch` MUST list remote branches containing the candidate (`git branch -r --contains <candidate> --list 'origin/release/*'`) and return: the branch name (without `origin/`), `""` (on no `release/*` branch: cut outside the model, or already merged back and deleted), or *unknown* (git failed). It MUST NOT be recomputed from the tag's date (a cycle opened at 23 h and re-cut after midnight would be looked for under a name nobody used). Both `""` and *unknown* stop the merge path and are logged apart ("git could not say which branch it is on" vs "it is on no release/* branch"); neither ever licenses a repository-wide search for the release PR.
Source: `release_advance.py:514-558,1709-1733`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:1843,1855,2021`.

### 2.4 Gate reading (facts the CI publishes; what the bridge reads)

**REQ-REL-034 — The gate is the run of the gate workflow on the candidate's exact SHA, its own tag ref, from the tag push.** The bridge MUST resolve the candidate's commit (`rev-list -n 1 <tag>`; failure → "the commit of `<tag>` could not be resolved"), list the gate workflow's runs (`pre-release-qa.yml`, limit 100: `headSha, headBranch, event, conclusion`), and take the **first** (newest) run with `headSha == sha`, `headBranch == candidate`, `event == "push"`. `conclusion == "success"` → green; any other conclusion (including empty = running or never concluded) → the conclusion is returned with detail "`<workflow>` concluded `<c or nothing yet>`"; no matching run → "`<workflow>` never ran on `<candidate>`"; an unreadable history, non-JSON, or non-list → empty conclusion with a reason. A run on `main` that shares a SHA is not a certification, and a `workflow_dispatch` re-run is not the tag's own verdict. A newer red run hides an older green one. `gate_green` is **only** `conclusion == "success"`; there is one reader of the gate's state and everything derives from it.
Source: `release_advance.py:561-648`; `.github/workflows/pre-release-qa.yml:565-620`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:547,798,807` (red gate stops the merge; unreadable history not green; a run that never happened is not green), `tests/test_release_qa_candidate.py:74` (a newer red run hides an older green).
Notes: In the target "gate green" for the GO is the gate of the **latest RC** (REQ-REL-066).

**REQ-REL-035 — The gate colour narrated by `release status` is an allowlist.** Only GitHub's `success` is `green`; `failure`, `timed_out`, `startup_failure`, `cancelled`, `action_required` are `red`; **everything else, empty or newly invented is `orange` ("not decided yet")** — never falls through to green. An unbuildable GitHub identity, no candidate yet, or an unreadable gate all land on `orange` with the reason.
Source: `release_audio.py:124-188`; `tests/test_release_audio.py:133,147,167,180`.

**REQ-REL-036 — A red gate's detail is read once, only to alert.** Only when the GO is false because the gate is not green AND a stagnation alert is about to be sent, the bridge MAY read the gate run's URL (same three-part match) and the names of its jobs whose conclusion is not in `{success, "", skipped, neutral}` (`gh run view <id> --json jobs`). Best-effort: a failure to read either MUST NOT cost the alert; never on the per-tick check.
Source: `release_advance.py:651-780`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:2209-2260,2328`.
Notes: (Added 2026-10-08, the owner's decision on a red gate, BR-ASDLC-016.) The gate-fix ticket (spec 05 §3) also reads a red gate's detail: the failed suites of `results/suites.json` in its evidence pack, once per candidate, when the ticket is opened; a failure to read them costs neither the ticket nor the alert (the ticket says the suites are unknown).

**REQ-REL-037 — The gate workflow publishes only facts, and a failed attempt earns at most one rerun by a pure decision.** The gate workflow (CI, not the bridge) MUST: run on `push: tags: <candidate glob>`; resolve the class (REQ-REL-010); write `results/suites.json` (`{failed:[…], suites:[{name, outcome, flaky?}]}`) and a QA report into the evidence pack uploaded as artifact `release-gate-evidence-<sha>` (missing pack = failure); fail the run if any suite is not green (a deterministic failure is never a "non-blocking" ticket: it is a fix and a new RC); expose `failed_suites` and `summary_written` outputs (the latter distinguishes "everything passed" from "nothing ran"); have a `gate-status` job that fails unless the QA job **succeeded** (a skipped QA job must never produce a green gate); a `suites.json` that is missing is a red run; **never write to a tracker** (the brief, QA tracker, Telegram and fix-task steps of the Python workflow are dropped, §3).
The **rerun decision** (`gate_auto_rerun`, a pure function) MUST be: rerun iff (a) `summary_written` is false (runner abort: no summary at all) — checked first, whatever `failed_suites` says — or (b) a summary was written, names at least one failed suite, and **every** failed suite has a matching entry in `known-flakes.json` (`[{suite, ticket, expires: YYYY-MM-DD, note}]`) whose `expires >= today` (an entry expiring today is still valid; a malformed date or a different suite never matches). A written summary naming no failed suite is not a recognised case → no rerun. The known-flakes file read MUST fail closed: missing, invalid JSON, non-array → empty list (with a warning), non-object entries dropped; the shipped file is `[]`. The decision MUST be made on attempt 1 only, in a job that does not itself call the rerun API (GitHub refuses to rerun an in-progress run); the rerun itself is a separate `workflow_run`-triggered job that runs **trusted default-branch code**, requires the QA job to have failed, downloads the evidence by source run + SHA, and calls `rerun-failed-jobs` on the source run. The decision output MUST be a two-line `rerun=<true|false>` / `reason=<one line>`, whitespace (newlines included) collapsed so a hand-edited ticket value cannot inject a second `key=value` into `$GITHUB_OUTPUT`; it exits 0 even with a missing flakes file.
Source: `scripts/gate_auto_rerun.py:37-143`; `.github/release-gate/known-flakes.json:1`; `.github/workflows/pre-release-qa.yml:138-175,543-680`; `.github/workflows/release-gate-auto-rerun.yml`; `tests/test_gate_auto_rerun.py:41-256` (all 24 — see §4), `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:2597-2893`, `:562-660`.
Notes: Placement is open (§5): the decision MAY stay in the CI scripts or become `spl-asdlc ci gate-rerun-decision`; the rule is the contract either way. Telegram/fix-task jobs on a red gate are dropped; the bridge's gate-fix ticket (spec 05 §3, BR-ASDLC-016), opened once the rerun settled or `release.gate_rerun_wait_minutes` passed with no rerun seen, and its stagnation alert (REQ-REL-079) are what cover a red gate.

### 2.5 Release issue creation and brief

**REQ-REL-038 — Release item identity: title, label, marker.** For `(product, version)` the release item MUST be identifiable by: **title** `Release <product> v<X.Y.Z>`; **label** `release/<product>-v<X.Y.Z>` (the same label the candidate tag minus `-rc.N` yields; `0.4.90` and `v0.4.90` normalise to the same `v0.4.90`); a **description marker** (a stable machine-readable first line) so a release item is distinguishable from any ticket that merely carries the label. `find_release_item(product, version)` MUST match on the label **and** the exact (trimmed) title among top-level items carrying the label (every embarked ticket and finding also carries the label, and a finding is a top-level issue too); the label query returns at most 200 items and a full page without a match MUST log "the release item may be past the first page" rather than answer "not found" as if it were a fact. `open_release_items(product)` MUST return **every** open item whose title starts `Release <product> ` and whose description starts with the marker (never "the newest"), so an ambiguous multi-cycle state is named, not guessed. An unreadable version (`normalise_version("")` = `"v"`) MUST never be queried (it would ask for the label `release/<product>-v` and report a clean nothing).
Source: `release_service.py:72,124,499-527,1953-2057`; `tests/test_release_service.py:84` (label identical however the version is spelled), `tests/test_release_audio.py:84` (open-release discovery requires the canonical marker), `:582` (unreadable version never queries a bogus label), `:410` (returns every open cycle without choosing one).

**REQ-REL-039 — The bridge creates the release item idempotently at the first tick that sees the candidate; fail-closed.** For every candidate in flight (REQ-REL-031) the bridge MUST ensure the release item exists, in this order: resolve the team (config `team`; **refuse** "no team id for the release item" rather than guess); ensure the release **label** exists on the team (create it if missing); resolve the **initial state** (`Ready`) **before** creating the item and refuse if the team has no such state (a state-less create lands in the team default `Triage`, which no consumer polls); create the item with title, description (REQ-REL-040), the label, state `Ready`, priority **Urgent**; re-read it. If the item already exists for this version it MUST be **completed** (REQ-REL-048 creates any missing angle sub-items; labelling REQ-REL-043) and never duplicated: a re-detected `rc.N+1` of the same version, a crashed first tick, or two bridges running at once MUST end with exactly one release item. The item's creation MUST consume no more tracker items than the release item itself plus the sub-items REQ-REL-048 creates. Every failure raises out of the **creation** (no best-effort), but never out of the tick (REQ-REL-102): the candidate is reported as "release item could not be created" and retried next tick, and nothing else advances for it.
Source: `release_service.py:2107-2201` (`open_release`), `2204-2329` (`ensure_template`, idempotent completion); `linear_cli.py:361-426`; `tests/test_release_service.py:2062` (urgent), `:2076` (creates exactly the coordination issue and the tracker — two issues, no per-reviewer sub-issues), `:2127` (idempotent), `:2151` (no team id refuses), `:2162` (created in `Ready`), `:2177` (team without `Ready` refuses), `:4099` (completes a template a previous cut left half-written), `:4138` (no-op on a complete release).
Notes: Python wrote the issue **before** the tag was pushed; the target writes it after (REQ-REL-016). A race between two ticks creating the same item MUST be survivable: re-find by label+title after any create error before failing. The Free-plan quota archiving (`_archive_before_open`) and `usage limit exceeded` handling are tracker-specific and dropped (§3); a tracker that refuses creation for quota is an ordinary create failure.

**REQ-REL-040 — The release item description carries the goal, the release-note slot, the candidates log, the scope and the angles.** Rendered in English, in this order: marker; `## Goal` (deliver `<product> <version>` to production: green gate on the latest candidate, every angle's PR merged, no open blocker, release-please note covering the range, fixes in the candidate; **the GO belongs to nobody, it is computed**; nobody closes this item by hand, the closure does); `## Release note` (placeholder "to be completed before publication" — the generated block REQ-REL-093 replaces it); `## Candidates` (running log: one line per candidate `` `<tag>` — class `<class>` — first candidate``); `## Scope` (the release label that groups the release's issues, and a table angle / owner / scope globs, empty scope = "every candidate"). The description MUST NOT carry verdict checkboxes (§3: replaced by angle PRs).
Source: `release_service.py:564-619`; `tests/test_release_service.py:104` (template carries the sections), `:3894-3926` (template verb needs no identity; prints without touching the tracker).
Notes: The `release template` verb (print what would be created, no network, no identity) is retained as an operator tool (REQ-REL-097).

**REQ-REL-041 — Each new candidate appends a structured comment to the release item.** For every RC the bridge first sees, it MUST post (once per candidate, REQ-REL-100) a comment with: `` ### Candidate `<tag>` ``; class (or "unclassified"); previous candidate or "First candidate."; the gate run URL when known; "Paths touched since the previous candidate: N" with the first **20** paths and "… and M more"; the angle scope decision (REQ-REL-054): which angles are (re)opened for this candidate, which are in scope but already open, which are kept (out of scope). When nothing is reopened it MUST say "No review reopened: no review scope changed" — "no review was reopened" is as much a fact as a reopen. It MUST also append a line to the item's `## Candidates` log.
Source: `release_service.py:1321-1374,2523-2609`; `tests/test_release_service.py:1696` (comment says so when nothing reopened), `:1714` (names the scope that reopened each review), `:3576` (does not lie when an in-scope review is still open — a past incident), `:766`.

**REQ-REL-042 — Embarked tickets are the chain commit → PR → linked ticket; no text is parsed for an identifier.** For each commit of the candidate range (REQ-REL-018) in oldest-first order the bridge MUST:
1. list the merged PRs GitHub associates with the commit (`GET /repos/{slug}/commits/{sha}/pulls`, keeping only entries with `merged_at` and a number); a non-200, non-JSON or non-list answer is an **error** (never "no PR");
2. pick **the PR that merged it**: the one whose `merge_commit_sha` equals the commit (squash/merge commit), else the only merged PR associated with it (rebase); several and none exact → **no PR is named** rather than guessed (the commit stands as a "no PR" change);
3. skip a PR already seen (dedupe by number — a PR is one change however many commits it has);
4. for a non-bookkeeping PR (REQ-REL-026), ask the tracker for the tickets that **link that PR URL** (`Tracker::tickets_linked_to_pr`; an unreadable answer is an **error**, never "no ticket"), upper-case the identifiers, **keep only the release team's key** (`<TEAMKEY>-`, key derived from the release item's own identifier, so no extra read; an unreadable team key refuses: "cannot tell the release team's tickets"), sort by `(prefix, number)`.
The result is a list of **changes** `(commit, pr?, tickets[])` and `tickets_of(changes)` = `{ticket → [#PR…]}`. A change with no ticket and not bookkeeping is **untracked** and MUST be shown as it is (`PR #n`, or `commit <sha12>` when no PR merged it) in the brief and the note — never guessed into a ticket, never dropped. A lookup that fails makes the whole read fail (the brief is not posted rather than posted wrong). The identifiers MUST NOT be harvested from commit subjects, commit bodies, PR descriptions or ticket descriptions (`BR-AF-001` contains `AF-001`; `ABC-99999` appears in prose; v0.4.118 listed ten phantom tickets).
Source: `release_contracts.py:131-148,164-238,241-341`; `release_service.py:4163-4222` (the retired free-text `parse_tickets`: `(?<![A-Za-z0-9-])\b([A-Za-z]{2,5}-\d+)\b`, case-insensitive, upper-cased, team-key filter, empty key drops everything); `tests/test_release_ticket_notes.py:47-115` (merged PRs keeps only what GitHub says merged; a GitHub that does not answer is refused; a non-GitHub origin is refused; linked tickets asked by PR URL; an unread link is refused; the PR of a commit is the one that merged it; plumbing is bookkeeping), `tests/test_release_contracts.py:96-151,175` (lists with contract; off-team identifiers ignored; tickets are the ones the tracker links never the ones a subject names; unread link refused not read as no ticket; to-deploy judged though typed completed), `tests/test_release_service.py:858-913,1397,1514` (the retired parser), `tests/test_linear_cli.py:359-422`.
Notes: `tag_prefix` for the range comes from the shape (REQ-REL-027), and a bad declared shape is reported as an unreadable range, not a crash (`ContractsError`). The first candidate of a line that never shipped has no base and therefore an empty list, as its diff is; when no release was ever published for the line, the brief's diff falls back on the previous candidate and **the same base is used for the ticket list**, so the list and the diff above it agree (`previous_tag_is_release=false` ⇒ `since=previous_tag`). The team key comes from the release item's own identifier (`team_key_of`: `^([A-Za-z]{2,5})-\d+$`, upper-cased, no extra read); where no release item is at hand (the `contracts` preview) it is read from the team (`team_key_for(team)`: never raises, `""` when unreadable, and an empty key refuses the read).

**REQ-REL-043 — Embarked tickets are labelled with the release label, with two guards, idempotently, never raising.** At the first tick for a candidate and again on every later candidate (the whole range is re-read; the tickets an `rc.N+1` adds are labelled with the rest), the bridge MUST add the release label to each embarked ticket, because the closure only judges labelled tickets. Guards: (1) **team** — a PR can be linked from a ticket of any team; only tickets of the release's own team are in this release; (2) **state** — only a ticket the merge already moved forward (`ToDeploy`, or any `completed`-kind state) is tagged; a ticket still `InProgress`/`Ready`/backlog is *merely mentioned* and is skipped (logged at debug; the next candidate picks it up if its PR merges into the cycle). A ticket already carrying the label is counted and **left untouched (no write)**. A ticket that cannot be read, or whose team has no such label (`add_label` returns false), costs one ticket unlabelled for now — never the item, never the brief — and is not counted as tagged. The bridge MUST print/log a receipt `embarked since <base>: N ticket(s), M carry <label>`.
The range read for labelling at the **cut** may end at the **commit** the tag names (the tag may not be fetchable yet); once the tag exists, at the tag. A range that cannot be read yet (a product whose published tags the range reader does not recognise) MUST NOT stop the release item or the candidate record; the brief labels the same list again and **fails loudly** when it cannot.
Source: `release_service.py:4225-4310`; `linear_cli.py:490-538`; `tests/test_release_service.py:913-1046` (labels every one; skips an unreadable one and goes on; leaves a merely-mentioned in-progress ticket; leaves another team's ticket; labels a to-deploy ticket of the team; leaves an already-labelled ticket untouched), `:1151` (brief wiring), `:1215`, `:1245`, `:1274` (open records the candidate even when the range cannot be read).

**REQ-REL-044 — The brief is posted by the bridge for EVERY product, from GitHub facts.** Once per candidate, after the gate of that candidate is green (REQ-REL-046), the bridge MUST post on the release item a comment `` ### Release brief — `<candidate>` `` containing, in this order: commit SHA; class (or "unclassified"); "Diff since the last published release: `<tag>` — N file(s)" (or, with no release ever published, "no release published for this line — diff from the first known candidate `<tag>`"; or "`(first release)`"); "Diff since the previous candidate: `<tag>` — N file(s)" / "previous candidate not found — diff cannot be computed" / "first candidate of this version, identical to the diff above"; gate run URL; evidence-pack URL (`<run url>#artifacts`); **Tickets included** (the embarked tickets of REQ-REL-042, backticked, comma-separated; or "no ticket linked to the candidate's PRs"); **No linked ticket** (the untracked changes as named); **Business rules touched** (REQ-REL-045); two `<details>` blocks listing touched paths (since the last release; since the previous candidate), **at most 40 paths each** followed by "… and M more". The brief MUST NOT be a French/agent-queue artefact: it is the pack every angle agent reads, so none of them queries GitHub for it.
Source: `release_service.py:1377-1489`; `linear_cli.py:602-718`; `.github/workflows/pre-release-qa.yml:679-912` (the payload the workflow computed: `sha, previous_tag, previous_tag_is_release, previous_candidate_tag, previous_candidate_unknown, release_class, run_url, evidence_url, changed_paths, changed_paths_since_candidate`); `tests/test_release_service.py:834-858,1610-1696` (singled-out rule files; names tickets and rules; first candidate named as such; both diffs on a later candidate; distinguishes first from unresolvable; says when no real release stands under the cumulative diff), `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:414-562` (payload carries both diffs; names no previous candidate on the first; distinguishes missing from first; falls back when no release was ever published; **carries no free text for tickets**; survives a diff beyond one argv string).
Notes: In Python the brief was posted by the **gate workflow** for the single-component project only (a project without that job, so its tickets were never labelled and the closure judged nothing until a past incident moved labelling into `release open`). In the target the bridge computes the payload itself (it reads the tags, diffs and runs), so the 128 KiB `execve` argument limit that broke the brief of v0.4.119 (35 PR descriptions, ~195 KB) is gone by construction: values that grow with the release MUST NOT travel through process arguments.

**REQ-REL-045 — The brief names, per embarked ticket, its acceptance criteria and the business rules the release touches.** For each embarked ticket the brief MUST list: identifier, title, state, linked PR numbers, and its **acceptance criteria** — every checkbox line of the ticket (checked/unchecked, verbatim, with the open `## Post deploy` boxes marked as such) — and the **business-rule ids** it cites in its `Business rules` section. **Business-rule files touched** = the changed paths matching `docs/business-rules.md`, `**/business-rules.md`, `vault/**/business-rules.md` (configurable `[release].business_rule_paths`), via REQ-REL-012; when any is touched the brief says "each rule touched must have a named test that is green". The brief SHOULD link the evidence pack (the gate's `release-gate-evidence-<sha>` artifact, REQ-REL-037). A ticket the tracker cannot read is named and said unread, never described from something else.
Source: `release_service.py:1492-1503,1456-1468`; `release_contracts.py:401-471` (ticket contract reading); `business_rules.py:46-64,167-172` (`cited_ids`); `tests/test_release_service.py:834` (rule files singled out), `:841`.
Notes: Per-ticket AC/rule listing is **new** in the target (the Python brief listed identifiers only). Checkbox parsing is REQ-REL-087; `Business rules` section detection is REQ-REL-088.

**REQ-REL-046 — Brief timing, idempotence and failure.** The brief MUST be posted only for a candidate whose gate run is green (REQ-REL-034), once per candidate (idempotency key in REQ-REL-100: a retried step must not post the pack twice — in Python `comment` appends and is not deduplicated), **before** any side effect whose failure would leave a duplicate on retry (angle sub-item opening, REQ-REL-048, happens **before** the comment posts so a failure after the comment cannot cause a second post on retry). A range or tracker read that fails MUST make the whole brief fail (not posted; retried next tick) — never an empty or guessed list. The brief's facts MUST be read **before** the round opens, the evidence pack included (the artifact `release-gate-evidence-<sha>` listed, unexpired, on the green gate run; an unreadable list, an absent or an expired pack is a failed read): facts that cannot be read open no sub-item either, so that no agent starts without its brief (BR-ASDLC-004). When the release item does not exist the brief refuses ("the candidate was cut without one") and the bridge creates the item first (REQ-REL-039). The first whitespace-separated token of the first output line of the verb is the release item's identifier (a contract with callers).
Source: `linear_cli.py:602-718,673-708`; `tests/test_linear_cli.py:845` (stdout starts with the release identifier), `tests/test_release_service.py:1301-1482` (serves pending QA after a green gate; before posting the comment; ignores free text a payload may carry; line with no release reads the range the diff stands on; refused when the embarked tickets cannot be read; finds the instance's checkout when not told), `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:220-238` (brief posts on the issue; fails closed).

### 2.6 Angle sub-items, angle PRs and reports (target: replaces the Python QA tracker/verdict machinery)

**REQ-REL-047 — Angles are configured, never built in.** `[[release.angles]]` entries (`docs/spec/08-configuration.md`) carry `key` (unique, `[a-z0-9-]+`), optional `title` (default `<Key> review`), optional `assignee` (an opaque tracker user reference) and optional `notes` (the project's angle notes; the focus and checklist of an angle are its protocol file `docs/agents/angles/<key>.md`). An entry with no key makes the file invalid (never guessed, never skipped); **no angle configured → refuse** to open a release ("no release angle configured"): a release with no QA would compute its GO from zero reviews, and a built-in list would name the agents of one installation. Duplicate keys are a config error. The code carries no angle of its own.
Source: `release_service.py:203-226,250-256,413-493`; `tests/test_release_service.py:163-184` (vault table can replace the reviewers; an entry without an agent is dropped, not guessed), `:293-322` (unreadable table refuses instead of opening a release with no review; a table that names no reviewer is a config error; the code names no reviewer agent).
Notes: Python's `products.<p>` overrides, `release_agent`, `post_release_agent`, `linear_user_ids` and `scope_drift` are dropped (§3).

**REQ-REL-048 — The bridge creates one sub-item per angle per RC pass, only once the gate of that RC is green.** For the **latest** candidate, when its gate is green (REQ-REL-034), release item exists, and `rc ≤ max_rc_passes` (REQ-REL-063), the bridge MUST create, for each angle **in scope** (REQ-REL-054), a child of the release item titled `<angle title> — <product> v<version> (rc.<N>)`, with the release label, priority Urgent, assignee = the angle's `assignee` when configured (a missing assignee is a loud warning, not a failure), state `Ready` (servable), and a self-contained body: `## Goal` (render the review of angle X on candidate `<tag>`, with the angle `focus`); `## Non-goal` (another angle's review; the angles run in parallel); `## Acceptance criteria` as **real checkboxes** (never plain bullets: closure covers every child, so bullets read as a contract nobody declared) — open `qa/<candidate>/<angle>` from the release branch; commit blocking fixes **with a test each**; commit the mandatory report file (REQ-REL-051); open a PR to the release branch naming this sub-item; `## Context` (candidate, release label, checklist, scope, path of the report, link to the brief); `## What closes this item`, in the bridge's terms; and `## Protocol`, the release angle's protocol, the angle's file and the report's schema and rules, embedded at creation and never rewritten (BR-ASDLC-015). Creation MUST be idempotent (a sub-item for `(candidate, angle)` that exists is never duplicated: found by title+label or by an embedded marker, REQ-REL-100) and the sub-items MUST NOT be created, nor made `Ready`, before the gate is green (QA is not paid on a candidate the deterministic layer refused). A create that fails leaves the angle un-offered and the GO false (REQ-REL-066 reports it as a *missing angle*); the next tick retries.
Source: `release_service.py:2204-2465` (creation, fail-closed state resolution), `1157-1233` (sub-item body with real checkboxes and exit condition), `99-109` (QA starts in a pending state until the gate is green, `serve_pending_qa` at `3391-3419`); `tests/test_release_service.py:115` (a QA sub-issue carries acceptance criteria — not decoration), `:2557,:2578` (serve pending promotes backlog children; idempotent), `:5616` (QA sub-issue criteria do not require their own closure), `tests/test_release_qa_launch.py:34` (a green gate starts the QA once per candidate).
Notes: Python had a *shared QA tracker child* plus `release_qa::<key>` verdict checkboxes on the release item, relayed reviewer to reviewer; that is dropped. In the target the verdict is the **merged angle PR** (REQ-REL-052) and the sub-item is the agent's work item.
Completed by the specification (2026-10-09, `docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §3–§5, BR-ASDLC-020): the candidate's consolidation item is created with the sub-items and blocked by every one of them; each sub-item follows its angle PR (In Progress when it appears, In Review once ready and green, back In Progress on a coordinator's changes requested, Done at its merge); the consolidation item is the overall review, Done when a newer candidate is tagged or the release is published.

**REQ-REL-049 — Angle branch and PR conventions.** The branch MUST be `qa/<candidate>/<angle>` (candidate = the full RC tag), branched from the **release branch** (never from `main`); the PR's **base** MUST be the release branch of the cycle; the PR MUST name its sub-item (identifier in the title or body, or the tracker's native PR link) so the bridge can attach it; the PR title/body are English. The bridge MUST recognise an angle PR by head ref pattern + base, resolve `(candidate, angle)` from the ref, and ignore (log) a PR whose head ref matches but whose base is not the release branch or whose candidate is not the latest. A PR that fails the pattern is not an angle PR and is not merged by this path.
Source: target design; closest Python: `release_cycle.py:39,43` (record branch per candidate: "a re-cut rc.N+1 gets its own record, never an edit of the last one"), `pr_merge.py:1488-1520` (branch-prefix based recognition; unreadable head ≡ not an ordinary PR).

**REQ-REL-050 — The report file is mandatory and has a fixed schema; a malformed or absent report is a failed review, never a GO.** The angle PR MUST add exactly one report file at the configured path (default `qa-reports/<candidate>/<angle>.json`) matching schema v1:
```
{ "schema": 1, "candidate": "<tag>", "angle": "<key>",
  "verdict": "go" | "go-with-note" | "hold",
  "checked": ["what was verified, and how", …],          // non-empty (mandatory)
  "findings": [ { "id": "<angle>-<n>", "title": "…", "severity": "blocker"|"should-fix"|"nit",
                  "body": "…", "anchor": "file:line"?,   // anchor required for nits
                  "status": "fixed"|"open"|"rejected",
                  "fixed_in": "<commit sha>"?,           // required when status=fixed
                  "test_added": "<path::name>"?,         // required when status=fixed and severity=blocker
                  "reason": "…"? } ] }                    // required when status=rejected
```
The bridge MUST validate: schema/`candidate`/`angle` equal the PR's; `verdict` ∈ the three values; **`checked` non-empty** (it is what tells "found nothing" from "did nothing"; an answer with no `checked` is a failed review, never a GO); finding ids unique; `severity` ∈ the three; `status=fixed` ⇒ `fixed_in` is an ancestor of the PR head and (blocker) `test_added` names a path the PR adds/changes; `status=rejected` ⇒ non-empty `reason`; a `hold` verdict with **no** open blocker finding is malformed (a hold must name what holds); two contradictory answers (e.g. a draft `go` then a `hold`) are no answer. A report that fails validation is a **failed review**: the PR is not merged, a comment on the sub-item names the first violation, and the GO stays false.
Source: `release_qa.py:41-96,54-76` (contracts: `verdict`, mandatory non-empty `checked`, findings `title/severity/body`, `rejected[]` with mandatory `reason`), `release_qa.py:185-260` (parsing), `release_qa.py:561-611`; `release_service.py:930-1003` (verdict with no evidence refused before any write — a past incident), `release_qa.py:78` (`NOTHING_CHECKED`); `tests/test_release_qa.py:53-77,271-401` (parse accepts fence/line fallback and nested findings; consolidation returns what it rejected and why; an ambiguous answer is never a go; the same answer twice is one answer; a rejection without a reason fails closed; unknown or repeated id fails closed; every finding accounted for passes; a role that says nothing about what it checked fails; both contracts ask for `checked`), `tests/test_release_service.py:3429` (a verdict without evidence is refused before any write), `tests/test_linear_cli.py:535-603`.
Notes: The Python finding schema lives in the vault doc `release-finding-schema.md` (not in the repo); the JSON above is **reconstructed** from `release_qa.py`'s contract and the `blocker | should-fix | nit` vocabulary (§5, open question: JSON vs Markdown front-matter). `test_added` implements the Python rule "a finding that becomes a test leaves the checklist".
