**REQ-REL-051 — The coordinator approves; the bridge merges the angle PR on approval, with guards.** The bridge MUST merge an angle PR (squash or merge, configurable; **pinned with `--match-head-commit <head sha>`**) iff **all** hold: base = the cycle's release branch and head matches REQ-REL-049; the PR is **approved by a configured coordinator** (`[release].coordinators`, GitHub logins) on its **current head SHA** (an approval on an older head does not count); the report validates (REQ-REL-050) **at that head**; open blockers in the report do NOT hold the merge (`docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §5, `06-qa-report.md` rule 4): the angle PR is merged and each blocker it could not fix becomes a blocker ticket from the merged report (REQ-REL-052), which holds the GO; the PR's required checks are green (`none` is not green: checks not reported at all means nothing verified; `pending` waits); the PR is not draft and is not `CONFLICTING/DIRTY` (if `BEHIND`, update the branch **from the release branch** only — never from `main`); head is not a `release/**` or record branch (REQ-REL-017). It is an irreversible action (REQ-REL-071). On a failed merge call the PR stays; the sub-item gets a comment; retried next tick. After the merge the sub-item moves to `Done` and the merged report is the angle's recorded verdict.
Source: target design; closest Python: `release_advance.py:2379-2422` (record PR: author proof, check state red/pending/BEHIND/CLEAN, content verification, `--squash --match-head-commit`), `pr_merge.py:1194-1211` (`_check_state`: none ≠ green), `release_qa.py:561-590` (role verdicts settled from consolidation: a confirmed blocker holds its role).
Notes: Approval semantics, who is the coordinator (an agent identity or a human) and whether the bridge or the coordinator merges are defined by the target; the Python "consolidation" invariant (every role finding accounted for exactly once; a rejection needs a reason; a consolidation that drops a blocker fails closed) maps to REQ-REL-050's `status` + `reason` rules and to the coordinator's approval, and is flagged in §5.

**REQ-REL-052 — Findings of a merged report become tickets; blockers block, non-blocking relate.** After an angle PR is merged, the bridge MUST read the **merged** report and, for each finding with `status ∈ {open}`:
- `blocker` → a **blocker ticket** (REQ-REL-056) — title, body, the release label, `blocks` relation to the release item, marker in its own description;
- `should-fix` → its own ticket in `Triage`, labelled with the release label **and** `qa-finding`, related (`related`, not `blocks`) to the release item;
- `nit` → as `[release].nits` says (REQ-REL-061): one acceptance box of the release's **shared nits ticket** (`shared`, the default), or its own ticket in `Triage` (`per-nit`); either way a full contract whose goal is to triage the nit.
`status=fixed` findings produce no ticket (the fix and its test are in the merged PR); `status=rejected` produce none. The filing MUST be **idempotent** (REQ-REL-053/060/061): re-reading the same report, a crashed tick, or the next candidate reporting the same finding gets the ticket already filed back, not a copy (a copied blocker would keep the GO false after the original is fixed). Each ticket body links the angle PR, the report path and the finding id.
Source: `release_service.py:2704-2780` (`file_finding`), `2787-2809`, `2826-2904` (`file_nit`); `linear_cli.py:887-947`; `release_qa_cli.py:152-169` (`_file_findings`); `tests/test_release_service.py:2195-2262` (each finding is its own ticket and a related one carries `qa-finding`; a finding filed again returns the ticket already filed), `tests/test_linear_cli.py:439-471` (blocker → blocking; should-fix → non-blocking; nit → shared-nit path).

**REQ-REL-053 — Finding idempotence key.** A finding is "already filed" iff an item with the **same normalised title** (whitespace collapsed, case-folded), **the release label**, and the **same kind** exists, other than the release item itself: a **blocker** matches only if it is **still open** *and* marked against this release (once fixed and closed, the same title reported again is a regression — a new blocker); a **non-blocking** finding matches in **any** state, including canceled (one canceled in triage was refused and is not filed again). The bridge SHOULD additionally embed a hidden marker `<!-- spl-asdlc:finding release=<id> angle=<key> id=<finding id> -->` so identity survives a retitle.
Source: `release_service.py:2783-2809`; `tests/test_release_service.py:2229`.

**REQ-REL-054 — Angle scope: full QA by default; lighter later passes as an opt-in.** By default **every configured angle runs on every RC** up to `max_rc_passes` (`[release] later_passes = "full"`). With `later_passes = "gate-and-coordinator"` (`docs/spec/08-configuration.md`), the passes after the first offer no angle sub-item: the gate and the coordinator judge the new candidate, and the GO counts the angle PRs merged in the first pass (`docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §7-8). There is no per-angle path scope: an angle reviews the whole candidate (`docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §4). The candidate comment (REQ-REL-041) says which angles are offered for this candidate and, under the lighter policy, that none is.
Source: `release_service.py:222-226,2467-2507,2612-2701` (the Python narrowing by touched paths, replaced); `tests/test_release_service.py:508-711`, `:766`, `:3306-3388`, `:3457-3627` (the scope tests are carried only for the parts that survive: a reopened review is a new sub-item for the new RC; a review never started is never reopened).
Notes: Superseded by the editor's decision (`later_passes`); the Python `qa_scope = "diff"` and the per-angle `paths` are not carried.

**REQ-REL-055 — An angle with no merged PR is a reported gap, not silence.** For the latest RC the bridge MUST track, per configured angle, one of: `not offered` (sub-item missing), `offered` (sub-item exists, no PR), `in review` (PR open), `merged`, `failed review` (report invalid / PR closed unmerged). `not offered` and the others other than `merged` make the GO false (REQ-REL-066); the stagnation alert (REQ-REL-079) names them. A sub-item that is `Done` but whose PR is not merged is **not** a verdict. Closing a release closes every angle sub-item still open with the release (REQ-REL-086).
Source: `release_service.py:1844-1899,3212-3260` (`open_qa`, `missing_qa`; "a review that is not there was never offered to anyone, so nothing will ever close it"), `2204-2329`; `release_audio.py:249-297`; `tests/test_release_service.py:382` (a release with no QA sub-issue is not a GO), `:4151` (a release missing a review is not a GO), `tests/test_release_audio.py:560` (a review the release never carried still waits on a human).

### 2.7 Blockers and non-blocking findings

**REQ-REL-056 — A blocker is an open tracker item with a `blocks` relation to the release item, marked in its own description.** The bridge MUST represent a blocking finding as a ticket that (a) carries the release label, (b) **says it blocks in its own description**, written by the **same create** (the only atomic write available): a block `release_blocker:: <release item identifier>` followed by human text "this finding blocks the release above; the marker is written by the same mutation as the issue creation, the `blocks` relation is a second write that can fail; while the marker is there without the relation, the status verb re-lays the relation and refuses the GO; do not remove this line by hand: close the finding (or requalify it, REQ-REL-096)", and (c) has a `blocks` relation **blocker → release item** (the release item is the *blocked* one). The marker is read tolerantly (optional bold, backticks and whitespace around the identifier: ``release_blocker::\s*\**\s*`?([A-Z][A-Z0-9]{1,4}-\d+)`?``) and written plainly. `release.blockers` = identifiers of the items that hold a `blocks` arc towards the release item **and are not resolved**, where resolved means state kind `completed` or `canceled` and **an unknown state counts as open** (waiting costs a delay; shipping costs an incident). The arc is the source of truth; the marker is the receipt that says an arc is owed. Fail-closed: a marker with no arc costs a refused deployment; a missing arc with no marker would cost a shipped vulnerability.
Open **angle PRs** (REQ-REL-055) are the *other* representation of "not yet clear to ship" and are counted by the angle condition of the GO, not as blocker tickets. Blocking-fix-in-candidate is structural for fixes committed in the angle PR itself (REQ-REL-059).
Source: `release_service.py:126-199,1905-1996,2704-2780`; `tests/test_release_service.py:364` (a blocking issue is not a GO and feeds Blockers-Carried), `:4267` (a blocking finding carries its marker in the description), `:4296` (marker survives a human reformatting it), `:4305` (a finding whose relation fails is named, not silently orphaned: `FindingNotLinked`), `:4329` (a finding marked but unlinked keeps the GO false), `:3963` (the GO is computable on the graph the code actually writes), `:4009` (an arc recorded the wrong way round cannot deadlock a release).
Notes: **The `FindingNotLinked` contract:** when the create succeeded and the relation could not be laid, the operation MUST report the created identifier and MUST NOT be retried as a new filing (the filing would be duplicated and the first orphaned); the next reconciliation lays the relation. If the `Tracker` backend can create an item **with its relations in one call** (e.g. Livela), it SHOULD, and then the marker is a belt-and-braces; the marker is mandatory for backends that cannot.

**REQ-REL-057 — Relation writes: retried, direction-correct, and cycle-proof.** `relate(a, b, blocks)` means **a blocks b** (`b` ends up blocked). The write MUST retry up to 3 attempts (no backoff) and raise the **last** failure when none lands; a `related` relation is never cycle-checked (it carries no wait). Before every `blocks` write the blocker's own blockers MUST be walked, and the relation **refused** (never written, never retried) when it would close a loop, when `a == b`, when the walk exceeds its **depth bound** or **node budget** (unknown is not "no cycle found"), or when a blocker cannot be read ("a walk that could not prove the absence of a loop"). A refusal that the tracker explains by comment MUST explain itself **once per (issue, blocker)** however often retried, and a refusal about a *different* blocker is still posted. This is the one place a `blocks` relation is written.
Source: `linear_service.py:3819-3891` (`_relate`, `_unrelate`; `RELATE_ATTEMPTS = 3`; the no-cycle invariant, a past incident); `tests/test_release_service.py:4727-4930` (a relation that would close a loop is refused and never written; an issue may not block itself; a long chain that does not loop is written; a related relation is never cycle-checked; a loop deeper than the walk bound is refused; a walk that spends its node budget is refused; a blocker that cannot be read is refused; a refusal explains itself once; a refusal about a different blocker is still posted).
Notes: Specified here because the release item is the sink of `blocks` arcs and the GO would deadlock on a cycle. The depth/node constants are implementation choices; the **fail-closed on exhaustion** is the contract. A `Tracker` backend with native cycle protection MAY delegate.

**REQ-REL-058 — Reconciliation re-lays what a two-step write lost, before any GO.** Before every GO computation (and before closure) the bridge MUST run `reconcile_release`: (1) for every **open** item carrying the release label **and** marked against this release (REQ-REL-056) **whose arc is absent** (`not in release.blocking_arcs`), lay the `blocks` relation; an item that is already resolved is owed no arc (re-laying it every tick would be a write per release per tick for ever); an item that is the release item is skipped; (2) a relation that could not be laid → the identifier goes to `unlinked_blockers` (the GO is then false and the identifier appears in `blockers`/`Blockers-Carried`). It is a **write inside a read verb on purpose** (the status verb is the last thing that runs before a merge). It MUST be **idempotent: it writes only the arcs that are missing** — a healthy release causes one read, no write — and it re-reads the release only when it wrote something. A reconcile that itself raises (tracker read failure) MUST skip the repo for this tick with the reason, never compute a GO on a graph it could not repair.
Source: `release_service.py:2907-3147` (`Reconciliation`, `find_unlinked_blockers`, `reconcile_release`); `release_advance.py:1604-1613`; `tests/test_release_service.py:4369` (the repair pass lays the relation the finding was owed), `:4409` (writes nothing on a healthy release), `:4506` (close refuses a release whose graph it could not repair).
Notes: The Python reconcile also (a) re-laid the post-release check's arc, (b) withdrew a release arc posed on an open QA issue (a past incident). Both address child shapes that do not exist in the target (§3).

**REQ-REL-059 — A resolved blocker frees the release only if its fix is proven to be in the candidate.** A **resolved blocker** = an item marked against this release (REQ-REL-056) in a `completed`-kind state (`Done`, `ToDeploy`); `canceled` is "a finding that was not a defect" (no fix to find); an open one still blocks by itself. Read off the marker, not the arcs, so a blocker whose arc never landed is judged too. For each resolved blocker, the bridge MUST:
1. collect the PR URLs the **bridge itself recorded** on the ticket when it observed the merge (a `merged_pr:: <url>` marker comment authored by the bridge's tracker identity — a comment anyone can type proves nothing, so markers from other authors are ignored; target alternative: the ticket's tracker-linked PR whose merge the bridge observed);
2. none recorded → a **gap**: "`<id>`: resolved (`<state>`) with no recorded merged PR — nothing proves its fix is in `<candidate>`" (the way out is the ticket's own state: a finding that is not a defect is closed `Canceled`);
3. for each URL (**only** `https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/pull/<n>`; anything else is a gap "not a GitHub PR URL"): read `state, mergeCommit`; not `MERGED` or no merge commit → gap "it is not merged, state `<s>`"; unreadable/non-JSON/non-object → gap; then `contains(mergeCommit, latest RC)` (REQ-REL-019): false → gap "the fix (`<url>`, commit `<sha12>`) is not in `<candidate>`"; unknown (git error) → gap "could not be verified".
**Every** recorded PR must be in the candidate (a fix that took two PRs is in when both are). A candidate cut after the merge releases the ticket. The listing of resolved blockers failing (tracker read error) is **one gap** ("resolved blockers unreadable… their fixes cannot be verified in the candidate"), never an empty list. With no resolved blocker nothing is read from git or GitHub.
Source: `release_fixes.py:46-155`; `release_service.py:2993-3019`; `release_advance.py:1124-1161,1673-1679`; `tests/test_release_fixes.py:119-246` (13 tests), `tests/test_release_range.py:139-167`, `tests/test_release_advance.py:1194-1298`, `tests/test_release_service.py:6161-6207`.
Notes: In the target the fix of a blocker committed **in an angle PR** is in the candidate by construction once RC == branch tip (REQ-REL-070); this check remains for blockers fixed by any other PR (a human PR to the release branch after the RC, or a PR to the wrong branch). Python's trust anchor (`pr_merge.fleet_author_ids`) becomes "authored by the bridge's own tracker identity" (§5).

**REQ-REL-060 — Non-blocking findings are tickets in `Triage`, related to the release, left open by the closure.** A `should-fix` finding MUST become a ticket with the release label **and** the label `qa-finding` (it groups a release's findings and puts them first in refinement), `related` to the release item (never `blocks`), moved to `Triage`. Its create/relate/state steps are three writes; a failure after the create leaves a ticket that already carries the labels and is found by REQ-REL-053, so the next call returns it instead of repairing the missing relation (documented exposure; it can never make the GO wrong because `related` is not a blocker). The closure leaves such tickets in `Triage`/`Backlog` **on purpose** (REQ-REL-086).
Source: `release_service.py:2704-2780,2776-2779`, `274`; `tests/test_release_service.py:2195`, `:4174` (close leaves a legacy backlog finding alone), `:2654`.

**REQ-REL-061 — Nits are triaged under a full contract, grouped as the tracker allows.** (Corrected 2026-10-07 to `docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §6, which wins: the owner's decision on the nits contract, BR-ASDLC-012.) Whatever the grouping, the bridge MUST write the nits work as a **complete contract** that `ticket check` accepts: `## Goal` (triage: classify each nit as kept or dropped, never presume it fixed; a kept nit becomes real work, a dropped nit is closed with its reason; the judgement is the refiner's), `## Non-goals`, the nits as boxes under `## Acceptance criteria`, and `No new rule.` under `## Business rules`; no ticket is ever written as bare checkbox lines, and no runtime exempts one by its marker. Each nit is **one box** `- [ ] `<candidate>/<angle>/<finding id>` <summary> — where: `<anchor>` — context: <evidence>`: what was observed, where, and in what context, as the report gives them, each made inert and collapsed to **one line** (a multi-line body would never match its key on retry and would corrupt the checklist with loose continuation lines). `[release].nits = "shared"` (default, for a tracker that limits its ticket count): one ticket per release titled `Nits — <product> v<version>` (found by release label + exact title, created on first use, related to the release item, in `Triage`); a later nit's box is inserted at the end of its `## Acceptance criteria`. Identity is the box's **key** (`<candidate>/<angle>/<finding id>`, spec 06 rule 5), not its checked state, its literal prefix (`- [ ] `, `- [x] `, `- [X] `) or the text after it: a nit already present — ticked, or given a reason after its key — is not re-added. Insert only: the description is read then rewritten whole, and content this call did not write (a human's prose, blank lines) MUST survive untouched. A shared ticket that carries no contract (the older format of bare lines, or a contract broken by hand) MUST be left alone, fail-closed: nothing is written into it and the tick reports it, at each tick, until it has a contract. A failed append is reported and retried. Two concurrent first-nits may create two tickets (documented, harmless to the GO: the relation is `related`). `[release].nits = "per-nit"` (for a tracker without that limit): one ticket per nit, in `Triage`, related to the release item, carrying the finding marker and found again by it like a `qa-finding` ticket (REQ-REL-060), without the `qa-finding` label.
Source: `release_service.py:524-528,1036-1051,2812-2904`; `tests/test_release_service.py:2262-2424` (a nit creates one shared ticket and further nits append; filing the same nit twice does not duplicate; a multiline body is collapsed and idempotent; a ticked-off nit is not re-added; appending never reformats content it did not write; a nit without a team id refuses; a failed append raises), `tests/test_release_audio.py:510,598` (`nit_entries` reads the shape `file_nit` writes; no nits ticket means no open nit).
Notes: Grouping nits was the PM decision of 2026-09-04; the target makes it a per-tracker setting (`[release].nits`, `docs/spec/08-configuration.md`), the shared ticket staying the default. Open question 10 is settled by it.

**REQ-REL-062 — Severity counts for status.** `count_findings(release)` MUST return `None` (not three zeros) when **nothing at all was filed** — "0 blocking" would claim a clean record nobody signed — else `{blocking, should_fix, details, blocking_details[]}`: `blocking` = open blocker items (marked against this release, or in `release.blockers`) as `<id> — <title>`; `should_fix` = open items labelled `qa-finding` that are not blockers; `details` = unchecked boxes on the shared nits ticket, or open per-nit tickets (0 when no nits ticket exists or an unreadable version). Only blockers carry their text. The status section on findings appears as soon as reviews land (findings are what a verdict is rendered **from**), not once a verdict box is ticked. (Python's `open_findings(release)` — unchecked `release_finding::` lines on the QA tracker, or `None` before a past incident — is dropped with the tracker; its target counterpart is this count of open finding tickets, which the closure comment also uses to say that the backlog stays open on purpose, REQ-REL-086.)
Source: `release_service.py:1054-1154`; `release_audio.py:107-145,289-298`; `tests/test_release_audio.py:106,437,463,491,505,611`.

### 2.8 RC passes and `max_rc_passes`

**REQ-REL-063 — Full QA on every RC, up to `max_rc_passes` (default 2).** A **pass** is one candidate (`rc.N`, N = the RC number). `max_rc_passes` MUST be configurable (`[release].max_rc_passes`), a positive integer; a missing value is **2**, a non-numeric or non-positive value makes the file invalid (`docs/spec/08-configuration.md` rule 7). Each pass, the angles are offered in full (REQ-REL-048/054) and every angle PR of that pass must merge. The bridge MUST NOT create angle sub-items for an RC whose number exceeds `max_rc_passes`.
Source: `release_qa_launch.py:38-68`; `tests/test_release_qa_launch.py:20` (rc passes are configurable and default to two), `tests/test_release_qa_cli.py:221` (the last allowed candidate with a blocker goes to a human), `:231` (an earlier candidate with a blocker does not escalate); `scripts/cut-rc.sh:594-601`.

**REQ-REL-064 — When the passes are spent with a blocker left, the release goes to a human, once.** On the QA of the pass `N ≥ max_rc_passes`, if an open blocker remains **or** the release branch moved after the RC (fixes were merged that no gate/QA has seen, REQ-REL-070), the bridge MUST hand the release to the escalation human: (1) assign the release item to the owner (`[owner] tracker_user`), (2) add the configured escalation label (default `blocked-by-human`, `docs/spec/03-vocabulary.md`), (3) post **once** a comment titled "Release-candidate passes exhausted — `<candidate>`" saying "candidate N of M allowed, and K blocker(s) remain" (when the cause is a moved branch: "fixes landed after the last candidate"), listing each blocker/angle, and "to decide: reduce the release's scope, allow one more candidate, or abandon the cycle; remove the label once decided", ending with the hidden marker `<!-- spl-asdlc:qa-escalation candidate=<tag> -->`. The hand-off is **idempotent over the whole hand-off, not one of its writes**: assignment and label are no-ops when already present, the comment is posted unless a comment with the marker already exists — a retry after a partial failure completes what is missing and posts nothing twice. An earlier pass with a blocker does **not** escalate (another RC is allowed). Authorising one more pass is a human act (raise `max_rc_passes` or an override, §5).
Source: `release_qa_cli.py:209-258` (`rc_number`, `_escalation`, `escalate`); `release_advance.py:1525-1541`; `tests/test_release_qa_cli.py:221-298` (last allowed candidate goes to a human; earlier does not; escalation assigns, labels, explains; not explained twice; a retry after a failed comment still explains), `tests/test_release_qa_launch.py:130-176` (QA that died without a verdict is retried then handed over; a hand-over that fails is retried until it goes through — acknowledged only once it succeeded).
Notes: BR-AF-055 — "two human interventions" in the chain: opening the cycle and this escalation.

**REQ-REL-065 — The bridge cuts the next candidate when the previous pass is fully merged and the branch moved.** When, for the latest RC (number `N < max_rc_passes`), every offered angle PR is merged (or the pass owes none), the gate is green, no angle is `failed review`, and the release branch tip is ahead of that RC's commit (fixes were merged), the bridge MUST dispatch the cut workflow with `action=candidate` (REQ-REL-001) — once per `(cycle, N)` (REQ-REL-100) — and MUST NOT cut a candidate while a release-please PR merge is in flight or while a pass is still open. It MUST NOT cut when `N ≥ max_rc_passes` (REQ-REL-064). A dispatch that fails is retried next tick and is not marked done.
Source: target design (Python: a human or `ci-autorelease cut` ran `cut-rc.sh` for `rc.N+1` after a hotfix merged: `scripts/cut-rc.sh:1960`-style flow; `docs/RELEASE_PROCESS.md` "Les deux gestes").
Notes: Open question (§5): whether the bridge or a human triggers `candidate`.

### 2.9 GO computation

**REQ-REL-066 — The GO is a pure computation over facts; nobody gives it.** `evaluate_go(release, facts) → GoDecision` MUST be a pure function (no I/O: the facts are established by the callers and passed in). GO is true iff **all** hold, each failing condition adding one English reason:
**Completed by the specification** (`docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §8): a seventh condition, the staged version equals the version the QA judged (REQ-REL-073), is part of the GO (`version_gaps`); an unreadable staged version is a gap. Under `go_policy = "human"` the owner's approval is one more condition, and an approval that could not be read never passes.
1. **Gate green** on the **latest RC** (REQ-REL-034). Unknown (`unknown` input / unreadable) is **not** green.
2. **Every configured angle has a merged angle PR for the latest RC** (REQ-REL-055). At least one angle must exist: a release carrying no angle at all is a template that never ran → refuse ("the release carries no angle sub-item and no verdict"). Angles the template promises but the release does not carry are **`missing_angles`** (never offered → nothing will ever close them) and are reported apart from `open_angles` (offered, not merged). "Every review that exists is Done" is **not** "every review is Done": the expected list comes from the config, not from the children that happen to exist.
3. **Zero open blockers** (REQ-REL-056), including `unlinked_blockers` that reconciliation could not repair (REQ-REL-058), de-duplicated and counted in `blockers`.
4. **The release-please note covers the candidate's range** (REQ-REL-023); empty `note_gaps` means covered **or** no release-please PR yet (nothing to judge); a note that cannot be read is **one** gap, not an empty list.
5. **Resolved blockers' fixes are in the candidate** (REQ-REL-059): empty `fix_gaps`.
6. **The latest RC is the release branch tip, modulo bookkeeping** (REQ-REL-070).
Inputs `unlinked_blockers`, `note_gaps`, `fix_gaps`, `tip_gaps` are **passed in**, never derived inside, so the function stays a pure reading of facts someone else established and a release read through an older query shape cannot look broken to it. The post-release work (Post Deploy tickets) is **not** a precondition of the GO: it is served after the deployment, so requiring it would deadlock the release against itself.
Source: `release_service.py:3153-3287,1844-1899`; `tests/test_release_service.py:331-496` (all three conditions hold → GO; red gate; open QA; blocking issue feeds Blockers-Carried; no QA sub-issue is not a GO; the post-release check does not hold the release hostage; note omitting commits is not a GO and names them; covering note leaves GO alone; unreadable note is a gap; very incomplete note does not flood the reason; note gap adds to the other reasons; serialises for the deployment guard), `:4151`, `:6161-6178`.
Notes: Python had five conditions (gate, verdicts, blockers, note a past incident, fixes a past incident). The sixth is new in the target and comes from the publication guard `release-publish.yml:140-188` (REQ-REL-070). "Open angles" replace "open verdict checkboxes / QA sub-issues not Done".

**REQ-REL-067 — The GO result carries its facts and its reasons; `Blockers-Carried` is computed.** `GoDecision` MUST carry: `go`, `gate_green`, `open_angles[]`, `missing_angles[]`, `blockers[]`, `unlinked_blockers[]`, `note_gaps[]`, `fix_gaps[]`, `tip_gaps[]`, `release` (identifier), `reasons[]` (never empty when `go` is false) and a serialisable form (`as_dict`). `blockers_carried` = `"none"` when `blockers` is empty, else the blockers joined by `, ` (a record claiming `none` against a non-empty list fails publication closed); it is the **only** value the gate record's `Blockers-Carried:` line may hold (REQ-REL-078), never typed by a person. Reason sentences (English): "the CI gate is not green"; "the release carries no angle sub-item and no verdict"; "angles missing: …"; "angles not merged: …"; "open blocking issues: …"; "findings marked as blocking whose `blocks` relation is missing and could not be re-laid: …"; "the release-please note does not cover the candidate's range (merging its PR would make it final): …" with the gaps **bounded to the first 10** joined by `; ` and " ; … and N more" (the full list stays in `note_gaps`); "resolved blockers whose fix is not in the candidate (a new candidate that contains it frees them): …" joined by `; `; "the candidate is not the release branch tip: …". Duplicates in each input list are removed preserving order.
Source: `release_service.py:1874-1899,3240-3301`; `tests/test_release_service.py:439-496` (`test_go_decision_serialises_for_the_deployment_guard`, `test_a_very_incomplete_note_does_not_flood_the_reason`), `tests/test_release_advance.py:2209-2260`.

**REQ-REL-068 — Per-tick evaluation order for a candidate in flight.** For each repo and tick: (1) read shape (unreadable → skip with reason) and candidate in flight (REQ-REL-031; none and a declared shape → REQ-REL-082 finished-cycle check); (2) require the tracker (no tracker identity/credentials → skip "no tracker identity for the release path"); (3) find the release item (tracker error → skip "tracker could not be read"; none → create it, REQ-REL-039; already `Done` → skip); (4) **reconcile** (REQ-REL-058); (5) read the gate (REQ-REL-034) — a not-green gate is logged at info; (6) open/serve angles when the gate is green (REQ-REL-048) and process angle PRs (REQ-REL-051/052); (7) read the candidate's release branch and the release-please PR (single shape only, REQ-REL-030) **before** the GO, because the note is a GO condition; judge the note at the PR head (REQ-REL-023); (8) compute `fix_gaps` (REQ-REL-059) and `tip_gaps` (REQ-REL-070); (9) `evaluate_go`; (10) if not GO → HOLD statement (REQ-REL-069), then redispatch/stagnation (REQ-REL-075/079) and return; (11) if GO → merge phase (REQ-REL-072/076-078). Each tick re-evaluates from scratch: the GO is **recomputed at every tick** and the PR is merged by code the moment it becomes true; there is nothing to tick by hand. Decisions are `merge | wait | stagnant | close | skip` with a reason; a bad repo costs only its own repo.
Source: `release_advance.py:1544-1845,2772-2804`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:445-556,817-849,1704` (the heartbeat runs the pass after the merge pass: order matters), `:576` (only one merge per tick).

**REQ-REL-069 — A computed HOLD is said on the release item, once per candidate and reason set.** When the GO is false **and every angle has answered** (`open_angles` and `missing_angles` empty) **and** an *answer* holds the release (`blockers` or `fix_gaps` non-empty), the bridge MUST comment on the release item "**HOLD computed on `<candidate>`** — the release PRs stay open" listing the reasons, `Blockers-Carried: <value>`, and "the GO is recomputed at every tick and the PRs are merged by code as soon as it is true; nothing to tick by hand". A gate or note that could not be read this tick is a **transient**, and a HOLD said on a transient is false: it MUST NOT be said. A review still under way MUST NOT be called a HOLD (saying so at each closing sub-item would bury the one comment that matters). Dedupe key: **the sorted `blockers` + sorted `fix_gaps`**, remembered **per candidate as a list of every key already said** (a blocker list that flaps between two states is said twice, not at every flip). A comment write failure never fails the tick and is not remembered as said.
Source: `release_advance.py:2584-2652`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:501-539` (a computed hold is said once; a hold whose reasons change is said again; a gate that could not be read is not called a hold; a hold that flaps back is not said again; a release still under review is not called a hold), `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:444` (a hold merges nothing, writes no record, says so).

**REQ-REL-070 — The GO requires the latest RC to be the release branch tip, modulo release bookkeeping.** (New as a GO condition; in Python the check lived only in the publication workflow.) The commit of the latest RC MUST equal the tip of the cycle's release branch, **or** every file changed between them (`git diff --name-only <rc sha> origin/<branch>`) MUST be in the configured bookkeeping set (`[release].bookkeeping_paths`, e.g. `.release-please-manifest.json`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `pyproject.toml`, the package `__init__`, the per-RC record `docs/releases/<rc>.md`). Anything else "landed after `<rc>` that the gate never saw": `tip_gaps` names the paths, the GO is false, and a new candidate frees it (REQ-REL-065). This is what makes **blocking-fix-in-candidate structural**: a fix merged to the release branch is either in the latest RC or blocks the GO. Read failures (branch tip, diff) are a gap, never "equal".
Source: `.github/workflows/release-publish.yml:140-188` ("Verify this release was QA'd": newest `<release>-rc.*` merged into HEAD, its gate run green, and `git diff --name-only $RC_SHA HEAD` limited to the bookkeeping regex); `docs/RELEASE_PROCESS.md` §"Branche de release courte" item 4.
Notes: The publication workflow MAY keep this guard as a second line (it also covers `workflow_dispatch` recovery which skips it).

### 2.10 Merge of the release-please PR(s) (single and multi-component)

**REQ-REL-071 — At most one irreversible action per repository per tick; one broken repo costs only itself.** Per repo per tick the bridge MAY perform **one** of: merge the release-please PR; merge a gate-record PR; merge an angle PR; run the closure. Everything else is a read, a comment or a dispatch. When two release-please-looking PRs are open, the tick still costs exactly one merge. The pass MUST never raise out of a repo: an exception for a repo is logged and becomes `skip: the pass failed (<exc>)` for that repo alone. The pass never asks a model anything: every branch is a comparison over GitHub output, git refs and a tracker query (zero tokens, safe at every tick).
Source: `release_advance.py:1-46,2772-2804`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:576` (only one merge happens per tick), `:556` (idempotent once the release PR is gone: the next tick finds no PR and does nothing, no error), `:1782`.

**REQ-REL-072 — Single-component merge: squash, pinned to the judged head, only on a computed GO.** With `GO` true, the release branch known (REQ-REL-033), and **the** release PR found (REQ-REL-030) the bridge MUST `gh pr merge <n> --squash --match-head-commit <pull.head_sha>`: squashing is what makes release-please recognise its own commit and cut the GitHub release; pinning is what makes a head that moved since the note was judged be refused by GitHub (re-read next tick) instead of merged with a note nobody read. The branch is **not** deleted (release-please recreates it; deleting would race its next run). Preconditions and outcomes:
- GO true but the branch is `None`/`""` → `skip` "the release branch of the candidate could not be established (<git could not say which branch it is on | it is on no release/* branch>)";
- no PR and `read_ok` false (gh could not answer) → `skip` "GO computed, but the release-please PR could not be read" (a transient read is not a fact to act on; redispatching would spend the candidate's one redispatch on a PR that was healthy);
- no PR, `read_ok`, `shape_seen` false → one redispatch (REQ-REL-075) → `wait`; otherwise `skip` "GO computed, but no release-please PR is open";
- a stale PR (`merge_state == BEHIND`) → one redispatch → `wait`;
- staged version mismatch → REQ-REL-073;
- merge call non-zero → `skip` "gh refused the release merge (exit N)";
- success → journal (REQ-REL-080), **mark the candidate as already redispatched at the moment of merge** (REQ-REL-075: a transient read right after the merge must never be mistaken for release-please having failed), notify (REQ-REL-080), decision `merge`.
Source: `release_advance.py:1654-1845`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:445-479` (a computed GO squash-merges the release PR; one line in the journal; says so), `:479-556` (open QA / open blocker / red gate stop the merge), `:915` (merge pinned to the judged commit), `:1553` (a gate read blip right after the merge never redispatches mid-publication), `:1704`.

**REQ-REL-073 — Staged-version guard: never publish a version nobody QA'd.** `staged_version(pull, product)` MUST return the normalised (`vX.Y.Z`) version of the **first** `<summary>\s*<product>\s*:\s*(\d+\.\d+\.\d+)` line of the PR body whose product equals the configured product (case-insensitive), or `""`. The merge MUST be refused when `staged != candidate's version` — an unreadable version (`""`) is a mismatch **by construction** — with `skip` "the release PR does not stage the QA'd version" and a detail "release-please stages `<staged | an unreadable version>` while the QA'd candidate is `<version>`". `main` keeps moving during QA, so release-please may have restaged `0.4.92` while the item under review is `v0.4.91`.
Source: `release_advance.py:137-141,1215-1225,1782-1792`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:699` (a release PR staging another version is not merged), `:713` (no readable version is not merged), `:1688` (the staged version is read off the body).

**REQ-REL-074 — A multi-component shape is never merged through the single-PR path.** If `shape.multi_component`, the pass MUST NOT apply the single-note coverage of REQ-REL-023 and MUST NOT read a single release PR; it goes to the cycle path (REQ-REL-076..078) once the GO is true and the branch is known. A GO that is true with no release branch is a skip, as for the single shape.
Source: `release_advance.py:1655-1671,1734-1740`.

**REQ-REL-075 — release-please is redispatched at most once per candidate, never on a read failure.** A candidate's `release-please.yml` MUST be dispatched (`gh workflow run release-please.yml --repo <owner/repo> --ref <release branch>`) when, **and only when**: the PR list was read successfully (`read_ok`) **and** (a PR exists with `merge_state == BEHIND` **or** no PR exists and no PR occupies the release-please shape under another author (`shape_seen` false)); **and** this candidate has not been redispatched already. A PR that is `CLEAN`/`UNSTABLE`/`BLOCKED`/anything else `mergeStateStatus` can answer is **not** stale. A shape-seen/wrong-author PR is not evidence release-please's own run is stuck and dispatching over it fixes nothing. The slug MUST be read off `origin` and match a **GitHub** origin only (`git@<host>:o/r`, `ssh|http(s)://[user@]github.com/o/r`, optional `.git` and embedded token userinfo); a local path, a non-GitHub host or no remote → "the repository slug could not be read off the origin remote" (never a plausible-looking wrong slug). The "already redispatched" mark is written **after** a successful dispatch (a failed write costs a duplicate dispatch, never a missed one) and also **at merge time** (REQ-REL-072); a **failed** dispatch is logged and retried next tick, **not** marked. This check runs on the GO-false path too (a hotfix pushed onto the release branch can make release-please's run fail before the GO is ever true) and **before** the stagnation clock, so a dispatch defers the first stuck message by one tick. Each dispatch is journalled (REQ-REL-080).
Source: `release_advance.py:1188-1212,1296-1380,2687-2704`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:1414-1626` (a behind PR is redispatched once and the pass waits; never twice; a missing PR is redispatched while a candidate waits and with a GO already computed; transient list failures never redispatch or spend the marker, including during the stagnation wait; a not-behind PR is never redispatched; slug read off the origin; fail-closed on a non-GitHub origin; fail-closed without an origin).

**REQ-REL-076 — Multi-component: read the cycle's release PRs; anomalies refuse the whole cycle.** `component_prs(prs, shape, base)` MUST consider the open PRs whose `base == release branch` and that have the release-please shape (label `autorelease: pending`, head prefix `release-please--`, **or** a title matching the component pattern). A shape-matching PR whose author is not in `shape.authors` (or whose `author_is_bot` is `False`) is ignored and logged (REQ-REL-030). Each remaining PR MUST have a title matching `^chore(\([^)]*\))?:\s*release\s+<component>\s+v?<X.Y.Z>\s*$` (case-insensitive; the title is the only place the version is written in a `separate-pull-requests` setup), whose branch — if it ends `--components--<c>` — names the **same** component, and `<component>` ∈ `shape.components`, at most one PR per component. **Any other anomaly refuses the whole cycle** (nothing is merged while one PR is unexplained): "#N has no readable component and version in its title"; "#N is titled for X but its branch is Y's"; "#N releases X, which `<product>` does not declare"; "two release PRs are open for X". The version component's PR missing → `skip` "no release PR is open for `<version_component>`"; its staged version ≠ candidate's → `skip` "the release PR does not stage the QA'd version". The open PR list failing → `skip` "the open PRs could not be read".
Source: `release_cycle.py:29-35,47-115`; `release_advance.py:2252-2305`; `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:370-433` (the other components merge before the version component; the version component merges once alone; a version component staging another version merges nothing; no version-component PR merges nothing; a release PR from another author is not a release PR; an undeclared component refuses the whole cycle; a merge is journaled with its component).

**REQ-REL-077 — Merge order: the record first, then the other components in declared order, the version component LAST.** The cycle closes when the **version component's** release is published (`shape.release_tag`); merged earlier it would close the cycle and the other components' PRs would never be looked at again. `merge_order` = the non-version components sorted by their index in `shape.components`, then the version component. One merge per tick: the first not-yet-merged PR in that order. Before **any** release PR the gate record must be on the release branch and attest this candidate (REQ-REL-078): **no PR is merged on a record the release branch does not carry**.
Source: `release_cycle.py:118-131`; `release_advance.py:2307-2353`; `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:370,380`.

**REQ-REL-078 — The gate record is written by code from the computed GO, through a PR the bridge opens, checks and merges.** `record_path(version)` (e.g. `docs/releases/v<X.Y.Z>.md`) MUST exist on the release branch with: the marker line `<!-- spl-asdlc:release-record candidate=<tag> -->` **as its own line**, **exactly one** line starting `Blockers-Carried:`, whose value (lowercased, trimmed) is `none` (`record_names`). Rendered content: `# Release v<X.Y.Z>`; marker; `- Candidate: <tag> (<sha12>) on <branch>`; `- Release item: [<id>](<url>)`; `- GO computed at <UTC "%Y-%m-%d %H:%M"> UTC by the release pass`; `Blockers-Carried: <value>` — **the GO's `blockers_carried`, never typed by anyone** (the deploy workflow reads exactly this line); `## Computed GO` explanation; the angle table `| angle | state |`; `## Release PRs of the cycle` listing `` - `<component>` <version>: #<n> `` in merge order. Landing it, in order:
1. record absent, or present but not naming **this** candidate (a record of an earlier candidate does not count) → `_land_record`;
2. a PR from head `release-record/<candidate>` onto the release branch already open → verify authorship (the release pass's identity in both spellings and `author_is_bot` not `False`; else `skip` "the gate record PR is not the release pass's own"); checks `red` → `skip`; `merge_state == BEHIND` → `gh pr update-branch <n>` (update **from its release-branch base**) and `wait`; not (`green` and `CLEAN`) → `wait`; then **verify it is exactly the record**: the files changed MUST equal `[record_path]` and the content **at the pinned head SHA** MUST satisfy `record_names(candidate)` (a commit pushed after the pass opened it must not land unreviewed; the ordinary merge pass never looks at this PR); only then merge `--squash --match-head-commit`; journal; decision `merge`;
3. otherwise create the record: through the **contents API** (no local checkout: the shared clone must not move), create branch `release-record/<candidate>` from the tip of the release branch (an existing head — a record PR closed by hand, a tick that died after creating it — is **deleted and recreated** from the base: its commits are nobody's the pass can vouch for), `PUT` the file, open the PR (title `docs(release): gate record <version> (<release id>)`, body naming the candidate, `Blockers-Carried`, the release item and "merged by the release pass once green; the release PRs of the cycle follow"), journal `release-record-open`, decision `wait`. Any step failing → `skip` with the reason.
The record branch is **named per candidate**: a re-cut `rc.N+1` gets its own record, never an edit of the last one.
Source: `release_cycle.py:39-43,134-196`; `release_advance.py:2307-2315,2356-2566`; `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:276-358` (a GO without a record opens the record PR and merges nothing; a record of an earlier candidate does not count; a green record PR is merged before any release PR; a record PR carrying another file is never merged; a record PR whose record was rewritten is never merged; a stale record branch is recreated from the release branch; a pending record PR waits; a record PR from anyone else is never merged), `:732` (the ordinary merge pass never takes a record PR), `:458` (a red gate merges nothing).
Notes: Only multi-component shapes have a record in Python; **in the target the record SHOULD be offered for every shape** if the deploy workflow reads it (open question §5).

**REQ-REL-079 — Stagnation: a GO false for more than `stagnation_hours` is reported once, by name.** `stagnation_hours` (`[release].stagnation_hours`, default **2**; a non-numeric or non-positive value makes the file invalid) is measured from the candidate's tag creation time (`creatordate`, which works for annotated and lightweight tags). If the tag date is unreadable, **no** stagnation warning is ever sent (logged). Younger → `wait`. Already reported for this candidate → `wait` ("stagnation already reported"). Otherwise the bridge MUST send **one** notice through the notifier (REQ-REL-095) and only on **success** record the candidate as reported (an unsent warning is retried next tick; a notifier with no destination configured logs an error and is not marked). Message content: "Release stuck: `<candidate>` has been waiting for more than N h."; **if the gate is red it is named first and apart** ("Candidate gate red (`<detail>`) — it is not the QA that blocks", failing jobs, run URL) — the angle lines below are not what holds the release back when the gate is red; one line per open **fix PR** against the release branch that is not release-please's own and not draft (`<url>` — held: "its CI is red" / "it conflicts with its base" / "labelled needs-review" / "reason not available") — read from the PR list already fetched, never a second look; open angles; missing angles; blockers; the note gaps (first 10, "… and N more") with the path to complete the note; fix gaps; and if none of those lists is non-empty, the remaining reasons **except** the gate line (said once); the release item identifier and URL. The extra gate/fix-PR reads happen **here only**, once per candidate. A decision `stagnant` is journalled with the lists.
Source: `release_advance.py:155-168,1228-1253,1259-1293,1406-1477,1499-1504,2655-2769`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:1317-1398,1630` (young stuck release not reported; reported once and names what is missing; unsent warning retried; no readable date never stagnant; no topic configured logs and is not marked; horizon configurable; defaults to two hours; the default notifier reaches the real sender with reasons and candidate), `:2209-2361` (red gate named before the QA sub-issues; message unchanged when green; red gate with no detail still says red; names failing suites and run link; the gate reason not duplicated in fallback bullets; fix PR named with its held reason when cheap; says so when not cheaply known; `fix_pr_reason` reads conflict and needs-review off the PR itself; `open_fix_prs` excludes release-please and other bases; with no base asks GitHub nothing; end-to-end red-gate and fix-PR warnings), `:1053-1086`, `:1230-1243`.
Notes: The Python dedupe store is `data/release_stagnation.json`; the target needs a durable equivalent (REQ-REL-100).

**REQ-REL-080 — Irreversible actions are journalled; the merge is announced.** Every merge, record-open, record-merge, redispatch, stagnation warning and closure MUST append one structured record to the bridge's action journal: `action` (`release-merge | release-record-open | release-record-merge | release-please-redispatch | release-stagnation-warning | release-close | angle-merge | candidate-cut`), `repo`, `pr`, `url`, `branch`, `candidate`, `version`, `release` item id, `component`/`component_version` when applicable, `merged_by: "bridge"`, and for the closure `closed`, `incomplete`, `awaiting_post_release`, `rules_unverified`. After a release-please merge the bridge MUST send a short notice (English): "Release `<version>` — publication started. Candidate `<tag>`: GO computed (gate green, all angle PRs merged, no open blocker, note covers the range, resolved blockers' fixes in the candidate). The release-please PR was squash-merged by the bridge. Release item: `<id>` — `<url>`"; for a component merge: "`<version>` — `<component> <comp version>` merged … PR #N squash-merged by the bridge; the deploy follows". A notice that cannot be sent costs the notice, never the merge.
Source: `release_advance.py:1386-1395,1813-1839,2326-2347,2570-2581,2751-2765`; `tests/test_release_advance.py:456` (one line to the action journal), `:469` (says so), `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:433` (a merge is journaled with its component).

### 2.11 Deployed ledger and merge-back detection

**REQ-REL-081 — The deploy ledger is read from GitHub: what the cycle embarked, and what is in production.** For a multi-component shape, once the cycle is finishing, the bridge MUST compute the ledger from the release tag: resolve `release_tag` to a commit (`rev-list -n 1`; not a 40-hex id → "could not be resolved to a commit"); `GET /repos/{slug}/commits/{sha}/pulls`; the cycle's branch = the `base.ref` of the PR whose `merge_commit_sha == sha`, which MUST start with `release/` (else "was not merged from a release PR on a release/* branch"); list the **merged** PRs on that base (`gh pr list --state merged --base <branch> --limit 500 --json number,title,labels`; a read failure → "could not be listed"; **a list that reaches the limit** → "more than 499 PRs merged on `<branch>`, the list may be cut": newest-first listing drops the cycle's *first* release PRs, a partial read must not pass for the whole ledger). An **embarked component** is a merged PR carrying a label starting `autorelease:` whose title matches `\brelease (?P<component>[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*) (?P<version>\d+\.\d+\.\d+\S*)` with `component ∈ shape.components`; it is **in production** iff it carries the label **`deployed: production`** (`DEPLOYED_LABEL`; set by the component's deploy job). No embarked release PR found, or any failed read → `None` ("I do not know what was embarked"), **never an empty ledger**. Entries are ordered by PR number. The ledger is read straight from the source because a merge-back does not prove it (`merge_back_only` skips the check; a DIRTY merge-back resolved off the branch never runs it).
Source: `release_advance.py:1866-1977`; `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:640-720` (the 29/09 case is not closed while the app is not in production; the app redeployed then closes the cycle; a component not embarked does not count; a ledger that cannot be read closes nothing).

**REQ-REL-082 — The cycle is over when published, merged back, and (multi-component) fully deployed — read off the refs, never assumed.** The closure trigger MUST require **all** of:
1. the newest candidate's release is **published** (`candidate_in_flight` answered "nothing in flight" for that reason; REQ-REL-031/032);
2. **no `release/*` branch holds the candidate any more** (`candidate_branch == ""`; *unknown* → skip "git could not say whether `<candidate>` is still on a release branch"; a branch still there → `wait` "`<tag>` is published; `<branch>` is not merged back into main yet" — merge-back deletes the branch only after merging it);
3. **the candidate AND the release tag are both ancestors of `origin/main`** (`merge-base --is-ancestor`: 0 yes, 1 no, anything else → skip "whether `<ref>` is in main could not be read"; a "no" → skip "`<ref>` is on no release branch and not in main: the cycle was not merged back, not closed" — the release tag is the proof that matters: release-please's version commit exists only on the release branch until the merge-back carries it over);
4. **multi-component only:** every embarked component of the ledger (REQ-REL-081) carries `deployed: production`; the ledger unreadable → skip "whether every embarked component is in production could not be read (<why>)"; any held → REQ-REL-083, `wait`.
For the **single-component** shape the trigger is items 1-3 (the release is "published" = its GitHub release exists). The tracker is read **after** the refs and **before** the ledger's GitHub reads, so once a release item is closed the following ticks cost no GitHub read. A release item already resolved (`Done`, or canceled by a person) is left alone: `skip` "the cycle is over and its release issue is already closed". Every failed read is "not this tick", **never "over"**. A closure the tracker refuses is retried at the next tick with the release item still open. After a successful closure the decision is `close`: "`<release tag>` published and merged back into main — `<id>` closed, N issue(s) Done".
Source: `release_advance.py:1851-1863,2064-2246`; `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:518-713` (a cycle merged back into main is closed by the pass; never closed twice; a release issue closed by hand is left alone; a cycle whose branch is still there is not closed; a branch deleted without its merge back is not a finished cycle; an unpublished candidate is never closed; an unreadable read is not this tick, never over; a refused closure is retried at the next tick; an issue already closed costs no GitHub read), `tests/test_release_advance.py:760-772` (a published release closes the question; a published agentforge release is not closed by the pass — [Python-only split, see §3]), `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:240` (a published version component ends the candidate in flight).
Notes: Python closed the single-component (AgentForge) cycle from the publish workflow (`ci-release close` after merge-back). The target unifies: the bridge closes both once it sees the facts. Python's CI also **filed a blocking issue against the release** when the merge-back failed, and a `closing-guard` job made "a published release whose closing never ran" loud (`release-publish.yml:1136-1260`; `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:3352,3420`). CI workflows never write to a tracker in the target, so the bridge replaces both: a release **published** for more than `stagnation_hours` whose branch is not merged back (or whose closure did not happen) MUST be reported **once** (tracker comment + notifier, REQ-REL-079/095) with the state the merge-back PR is in (`BEHIND`, `BLOCKED`, `DIRTY`, checks pending) and the recovery of REQ-REL-017; it never closes the release over a failed merge-back.
Corrected by the spec (2026-10-07, spec 05 §14): the closure runs only for a cycle with a release item of the bridge's; a version published with none (released before the bridge took the product over) is left alone — no item opened, no closure, no note.

**REQ-REL-083 — An embarked component that is not in production holds the closure and is said once.** When the ledger holds components without `deployed: production`, the bridge MUST comment on the release item **once per `(candidate, held set)`**: "**Cycle `<branch>` merged into `main`, but not in production** — the release item stays open" listing each `` `<component> <version> (PR #N)`: embarked, without the label `deployed: production` `` and the way out: either the component is redeployed (resume from the RC tag) so the label is set and the bridge closes the item at the next tick, or **a person decides to close without it** with `release close --version <v>` — "there is no automatic waiver". The dedupe key is the sorted `component#number` list; a different held set is said again; a comment failure costs the comment, never the tick. A component **not embarked** in the cycle does not count.
Source: `release_advance.py:1980-2046,2182-2191`; `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:640-713` (`test_an_undeployed_component_is_said_once`, `test_a_different_held_set_is_said_again`, `test_a_component_not_embarked_does_not_count`).
Notes: The manual `release close` verb is the human's only closure path (REQ-REL-097). PM decision 2026-09-30, option A.

### 2.12 Closure (per-ticket judgement, registry at the tag, Post Deploy, reopen/reassign, idempotence)

**REQ-REL-084 — Closure is triggered by the bridge, written by code, never by a person, and retried until it holds.** The closure (`close_release`) MUST be invoked by the bridge when REQ-REL-082 holds (or by the manual verb, REQ-REL-097). It MUST resolve `product` and `version` from the release label (`release/<product>-v<version>`: strip the `release/` prefix, `rpartition("-v")`); a label with no `-v` yields no product → the registry guard fails **closed** (REQ-REL-088), never silently off. The Python side effects on a "second writer" (Linear's team `autoCloseChildIssues`) are tracker-specific: a `Tracker` whose closing a parent would also close **children that must stay open** MUST expose that setting and the closure MUST refuse before any write while it is on (in the target no child must stay open: angle sub-items close with the release, so the check is a no-op; **a team with no id logs that the check is skipped**).
Source: `release_service.py:3723-3808`; `linear_cli.py:810-884`; `tests/test_release_service.py:2956` (close refuses while Linear would close the children itself), `:2981` (unset toggle treated as off), `:5160` (a label with no version fails the guard closed), `:5325` (a release with no team id says the auto-close check is skipped).

**REQ-REL-085 — The closure reconciles first and refuses to close over a graph it could not repair.** Before any member is judged the closure MUST run REQ-REL-058; if any `unlinked_blockers` remain it MUST raise "refusing to close `<id>`: the release graph could not be repaired (findings marked as blocking without a relation: …)" — closing over a marked blocking finding would mark `Done` something nobody read. Repairs made are named in the closing comment.
Source: `release_service.py:3769-3805`; `tests/test_release_service.py:4506`, `:5956`.

**REQ-REL-086 — Closure membership and what is done to each member.** Members = every item carrying the release label (≤ 200) **plus** the release item's children, each once, **excluding the release item**. In order, for each member:
1. **Angle sub-items** (children of the release item): if open → `Done` (they have no checklist of their own to fail; the queue has no job left once the release deploys); already resolved → untouched. The same for a consolidation item still open: the release pass moves it to `Done` when a newer candidate is tagged or the release is published (BR-ASDLC-020), so the closure is its fallback.
2. **State kind `triage` or `backlog`** (a non-blocking finding is filed there on purpose and carries the release label so the carry-forward sees it) → **kept open**; counted and said in the comment ("N non-blocking finding(s) remain in the backlog on purpose: …"). Sweeping it to `Done` would erase the backlog the review had just produced.
3. **State kind `completed` and state `Done`, or kind `canceled`** → untouched (a re-run is a no-op, not a wall of no-op writes).
4. **Every other member is an embarked/shipped ticket and is judged** (REQ-REL-087..090); it is read **wide** (full comment/history window) because the reassign decision depends on markers that are rarely among the last few comments.
Then the release item itself moves to `Done` if it is not already (never before every member was judged, REQ-REL-091).
Corrected by the specification (2026-10-07, `docs/spec/04-pr-review.md` §10, `docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §11): only an embarked ticket in `ToDeploy` or `PostDeploy` is judged. One `InProgress` or `InReview` shipped a merged PR that did not close it (every PR is independently deployable, BR-ASDLC-005): it carries the release label but is left in its state, for its next PR. Test: `a_merged_pr_ships_while_its_ticket_is_still_in_progress`.
Source: `release_service.py:3731-3775,3810-4012`; `tests/test_release_service.py:3225` (closure distinguishes gates from backlogs among three children), `:2993` (reads each ticket with the wide thread window), `:4174`, `:5408` (closes the QA queue with its release).
Notes: The legacy QA tracker transition (`_transition_qa_tracker`: the shared child turned into the post-release verification, label `Blocked by release`, a `blocks` arc, boxes ticked) and the post-release child sweep (`Vérification post-release`, `unblocked_post_release`, `interlocked_qa`) are **dropped**: each embarked ticket carries its own `## Post deploy` boxes and moves to `PostDeploy` (REQ-REL-090).

**REQ-REL-087 — Per-ticket judgement: the Done gate, then one of three outcomes.** For each embarked ticket the bridge MUST first apply the registry guard (REQ-REL-088), then evaluate the **Done gate** and map it:
- **Done gate** = every checkbox of the ticket **itself** plus every checkbox of every ticket connected to it by a `duplicate` relation (transitively; bounded walk: an exhausted walk is an *unreadable contract* and fails the gate) must be checked. A **checkbox line** is `^[ \t]*(?:[-*+]|\d+[.)])[ \t]+\[([ xX])\].*$` (multi-line mode) in the description with **fenced blocks removed** (``` or ~~~ fences, ≤3 leading spaces; **an unclosed fence is not a block**: it reads as text, so a stray ``` cannot hide every criterion below it), **heading-agnostic** (a box engages wherever it is written; a note that must not engage is a plain bullet, not a checkbox). The gate is **not ok** when: any box is open; the contract could not be read in full (`unreadable_reason`); or **no ticket in the duplicate closure declares a single box** (`no_criteria_declared` — the absence of a contract is not the contract fulfilled; fail-open on tickets that never had terms to meet was a past incident).
- **`## Post deploy` boxes**: the checkboxes inside a section whose heading text matches (whole text, case-insensitive) `^(?:(?:QA[ \t]+)?post[- ]?release|post[- ]?deploy)[ \t]*:?[ \t]*$` are *post-deploy items*, a **subset** of the open items (they still keep the Done gate not-ok); `release_blocking_items` = open items minus post-deploy ones (one occurrence removed per post-deploy item). A ticket **awaits post-release** iff it has post-deploy items, no release-blocking items, a readable contract and a declared contract; and **`waits_in_post_release`** iff it awaits post-release **and every one of those boxes is the ticket's OWN** (a duplicate's box cannot be ticked through this ticket's verb) **and** the ticket state is `ToDeploy` or already `PostDeploy`.
- **Mapping** (in this order): (1) `waits_in_post_release` → set state **`PostDeploy`** (no assignee change, no comment needed; counted `awaiting_post_release`); (2) gate **not ok** → **`InProgress` + unassigned** (REQ-REL-089, as corrected); (3) gate ok → **`Done`**.
Source: `release_service.py:3895-4007`; `linear_service.py:318-352,1225-1260,1405-1480,1531-1620` (checkbox regex; fences; `DoneGate`; `waits_in_post_release`; `done_gate_refusal`), `release_contracts.py:401-471`; `tests/test_release_service.py:2796-3225` (returns an incomplete contract to In Progress; reassigns to its author not the reviewer; …; closes a ticket with a complete AC and BR contract), `:6021-6146` (moves a ticket with only post-release boxes to Post deploy; a replayed closure leaves a post-deploy ticket where it is; no verification child needed; still reopens a ticket with a blocking box beside the post-release one; reopens a ticket that is not to-deploy even with only post-release boxes; still closes a ticket whose post-release boxes are all checked; reopens a ticket whose post-release box belongs to its duplicate; the hand-over mechanism is gone).
Notes: `Post deploy` is a `completed`-kind column: a replay sees it again and it must **stay** there, never be reopened. State names are the backend's (REQ-REL-098).

**REQ-REL-088 — Business-rule ids cited by a ticket are verified against the registry AT THE RELEASE TAG, fail-closed.** If a ticket's `Business rules` section cites any rule id, the bridge MUST read the product's registry **at the release tag this release publishes** (never a working tree, never an RC tag): the tag is `shape.release_tag(version)` (e.g. `app-v1.2.3`) when the shape declares one, else `<product>-v<version>`. The registry is `docs/business-rules.md` (configurable `[release].registry_path`) read via the GitHub contents API at that ref. Three answers: (a) **ids** — the set of rule ids; (b) **none** — the file is absent **at a tag that exists** → the guard is off for this closure, said at WARNING; (c) **unreadable** — no product/version in the label; no repository configured; shape unreadable; the read failed or the tag does not exist (the tag's existence is checked **before** a 404 is read as "no registry", so a wrongly computed tag fails closed instead of switching the guard off). Registry parsing: **declared ids** = ids in the first cell of a table row (`^\|\s*(BR-[A-Z]{2,6}-\d{2,4})\s*\|`, multi-line); a text with no such row falls back to every id in the file (a mention in a provenance column must not vouch for a rule). **Cited ids** = every `BR-[A-Z]{2,6}-\d{2,4}` in the bodies of every heading matching `^[*_\s]*(?:business\s+rules?|r[eè]gles?\s+m[eé]tiers?)(?![\w-])` (case-insensitive; `Business rules (ABC-617)`, `**Business rules**`, `Business rules:` and the French heading are the same section; `Business rulesets` is not). Judgement: **registry unreadable and the ticket cites a rule → left in its state** (neither `Done` nor reopened; recorded in `rules_unverified` as `(id, reason ≤200 chars)`; the next run judges it); **ids known** and the ticket cites an id not among them → **`InProgress`** with a comment naming the missing ids "(absent from `<path>` of `<product>` at this release's tag; if the id is a typo, fix it in `Business rules`; if the rule is new, open a PR adding it to the registry — it ships with the next release). Returned to `In Progress` by the release closure." and a mention of the ticket's agent when identifiable; **registry absent at an existing tag** → guard off, tickets go on to the Done gate. The registry is **read once**, lazily, only when some ticket cites a rule (a closure whose tickets cite none never reads it).
**Corrected by the specification** (`docs/spec/02-business-rules.md` §5): answer (b) below (registry absent at an existing tag → guard off) is not carried: a cited id must exist in the registry, so a ticket citing a rule of a product with no registry at the tag goes back to `In Progress` with the missing ids. Ids of another product's prefix are left to that product's registry, as at the merge.
Source: `release_service.py:3547-3721,3809-3938`; `business_rules.py:46-64,167-172,216-231`; `release_contracts.py:369-392` (the preview reads the registry at the **candidate**: absent only when the candidate exists and keeps none; any other failure raises, because the closure fails closed); `tests/test_release_service.py:4930-5325` (reopens a ticket citing a rule not in the registry; tags the agent the ticket is with [mention: tracker-specific]; closes a ticket citing a rule that exists; without a root says the guard could not run [dropped: instance root]; an unreadable registry fails closed on a ticket that cites a rule; a product with no registry at its tag closes and says the guard is off; a closure whose tickets cite no rule never reads the registry; a label with no version fails the guard closed; the closure finds a renamed product's checkout by its GitHub name [dropped]; the release table's checkout wins and the registry is read at the declared tag; a declared checkout that is not configured is said never guessed around [dropped]; a product GitHub does not know either still fails closed; a checkout named after its product asks GitHub nothing [dropped]), `tests/test_release_contracts.py:209,446-458`.
Notes: Product→repo resolution (`_product_repo_dir`, a past incident rename handling) collapses to "the repo this `spl-asdlc.toml` configures"; the **declared-shape tag** logic is kept.

**REQ-REL-089 — A ticket sent back is reopened AND unassigned, with a comment naming what is open (corrected by the specification below: the Python reassigned it to its implementer).** In the Python, when the Done gate is not ok (open boxes; unreadable contract; no criteria declared) the bridge MUST: (1) set the ticket to `InProgress` **together with** the assignee change (one write), (2) post a comment: the gate refusal text — "Cannot pass `Done`: N criteria/boxes unchecked, of which K carried over from `<dup>`" followed by up to **5** `[<source id>] <verbatim box line>` and "+M more", or "its contract (itself and its duplicates) could not be read in full (<reason>); an unreadable contract never promotes", or "no criterion declared: neither the ticket nor its duplicates ever declared a checkbox" — then "Returned to `In Progress` and reassigned to its implementer by the release closure" **or** "…and left unassigned — <reason>. The default implementer picks it up", plus a mention of the assignee when one exists. **Reassign = the ticket's implementer**, resolved via `Tracker::rework_assignee(ticket) → (assignee?, reason)`: the author recorded by the bridge/tracker (an `author::` marker written by the bridge, trusted comments only) first; else the actor of the transition into `InReview` **only if that actor is not the bridge itself**; never a reviewer, never the merge bot; an implementer who cannot be served (a human working from a terminal, nobody known) → **unassigned** with the reason (an unassigned `InProgress` ticket goes to the default implementer). "Reopened but left on the reviewer" made the reviewer fix the defect it had found (a past incident). The unknown-rules reopening (REQ-REL-088) uses the same state but no reassign.
**Corrected by the specification** (`docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §11; the owner's decision on `docs/open-points.md` #9; BR-ASDLC-009): a ticket the closure sends back to `InProgress`, for an unmet Done gate or for a cited rule the registry lacks, is **always unassigned**, in the same write as the state (`set_status(key, InProgress, Some(None))`). It is never kept on its current assignee and never handed to someone named: rework is distributed by being pickable, and the agent system resumes it by role. There is no `rework_assignee` and no `author::` marker. The comment is the gate refusal text (or the missing rule ids) followed by "Returned to `In Progress` by the release closure, unassigned: the agent system resumes it by role, with nobody named." A replay finds the ticket `InProgress`, judges it no more and writes nothing.
Source: `release_service.py:3939-4000`; `linear_service.py:7864-7895,1618-1680` (`rework_assignee`, `done_gate_refusal`); `tests/test_release_service.py:2796` (returns incomplete contract to In Progress), `:2841` (reassigns to its author, not the reviewer), `:2895` (reassigns to the real author when the merge pass flagged it), `:3040` (does not reassign to a host voice), `:3100` (leaves unassigned when its host-voice author has no assignee), `:3161` (unassigns when no author is identifiable).
Notes: "Agent identities" are dropped, and with the correction above so is "who implemented it": the bridge names nobody. The five comment text variants of Python collapse to one in English.

**REQ-REL-090 — `Post Deploy` tickets move to `Done` once every `## Post deploy` box is checked, written by the bridge at its tick (corrected by the specification below: the Python verb described here is not carried).** In the Python, `release post-release-check --issue <id> --box <text prefix> --evidence <text|->` MUST: refuse an empty box key or empty evidence **before any write**; read the ticket; require its state to be `PostDeploy` (or `Done`, only for the idempotent retry of a run that ticked the last box and closed it: then the box must already be checked, else refuse); find the boxes **inside the ticket's own `## Post deploy` section** whose text (markdown emphasis and case ignored) **starts with** the key — **exactly one** must match (none → "only a box of that section can be checked this way"; several → "give more of its text"); tick it by rewriting that line (`[ ]`→`[x]`, first occurrence) in the description (never a free-text report); **re-read what was persisted** and require the box to read back as checked; evaluate the Done gate on the ticket as it now reads: ok → comment "Post-deploy verification: box checked by the code — `- [x] <text>`. Observed: <evidence>. All boxes are checked: the ticket moves to `Done`." and set `Done`; not ok → comment naming the remaining boxes (≤5) and keep `PostDeploy`; an unreadable contract keeps `PostDeploy`. Idempotent: a retry finds the box ticked and still finishes the job without a second comment when nothing was newly written. The ticket is the **only** source of truth (no mirror line on any release child). Output: `<id> -> Done|PostDeploy`, then `still open: <line>` per remaining box. `--version` is accepted and ignored.
Source: `linear_service.py:1726-1830`; `linear_cli.py:970-1002`; `tests/test_linear_cli.py:920-970` (ticks the ticket and nothing else; says when it stays in post-release; needs evidence; reports a refusal; release close names a ticket moved to post-release), `tests/test_release_service.py:6146`.
**Corrected by the specification** (`docs/spec/03-vocabulary.md`, `05-release-cycle.md` §11, `07-the-bridge.md`, `09-requalification-and-human-gestures.md`; the owner's decision of 2026-10-07; BR-ASDLC-002, BR-ASDLC-003): there is **no** `release post-release-check` verb. A ticket in `PostDeploy` moves to `Done` by the checkbox mechanism: whoever verifies a `## Post deploy` box in production checks it on the ticket, and the bridge, at the tick that finds every box of that section checked (the Done gate, duplicates included; a read that fails never promotes), sets `Done` and removes the ticket's `post-deploy-validation/<product>` label; a ticket with one box still open stays in `PostDeploy` and nothing is written. When the closure moves a ticket into `PostDeploy` it labels it `post-deploy-validation/<product>` (the index the tick lists, since a tracker cannot be listed by status) and creates one **validation sub-task** under it: unassigned, in `Ready`, with a complete contract and a hidden marker (`spl-asdlc:post-deploy-validation parent=<id>`) by which it is found again among the ticket's children, so a lost answer is found and the sub-task never created twice; the tick creates it when it is missing. The sub-task asks its agent to verify and check the boxes, close the sub-task, and reassign it to the owner (`owner.tracker_user`) when a check needs a human or is beyond it. `PostDeploy` stays outside the in-progress group: the ticket itself is never served.

**REQ-REL-091 — Closure result, comment, unverified handling and idempotence.** `close_release` MUST return a `ReleaseCloseResult{closed[], incomplete[], awaiting_post_release[], rules_unverified[(id, reason)]}` (iterating/`len` still read `closed`). It MUST then post on the release item one comment ("Deployed. This item and the release's issues move to `Done` in code — N issue(s).") followed by the applicable paragraphs: backlog findings left in `Triage`/`Backlog`; issues left or put back `InProgress` (open boxes) named; issues left in their state because they cite a rule and the registry could not be read at the tag, "nothing was judged; re-running the closure judges them"; issues moved to `PostDeploy` ("their `## Post deploy` boxes are verified in production and checked on each ticket, as its validation sub-task asks; the bridge moves each ticket to `Done` at the tick that finds all its `## Post deploy` boxes checked" — corrected by the specification, REQ-REL-090: no verb); repairs made before closing. The verb prints `<id> closed; N issue(s) -> Done` then each closed identifier, each `incomplete` one ("left OPEN on purpose: its description still has an open checkbox (a ticket, not a gate — do not close by hand)"), each `awaiting_post_release`, and **exits non-zero when `rules_unverified` is non-empty** (so the caller says it; re-running judges them). The whole closure MUST be **idempotent**: a second run writes nothing for members already `Done`/`Canceled`/`PostDeploy`, never reopens a ticket a person already re-routed, and does not re-post a comment whose effects were already written.
**Target change (flagged §5):** because the bridge ticks every few minutes it SHOULD keep the **release item open** (not `Done`) while `rules_unverified` is non-empty and retry the judgement at the next ticks, notifying a human once if it persists past `stagnation_hours`; Python set the release `Done` regardless and notified once ("closed, but N ticket(s) left in their state… re-running the closure judges them") because no later tick re-ran the closure.
Source: `release_service.py:3423-3478,4009-4117`; `release_advance.py:2049-2061,2226-2246`; `linear_cli.py:838-884`; `tests/test_release_service.py:2745,2779` (names open findings; says nothing extra when none remain — legacy tracker), `tests/test_linear_cli.py:689-762,970` (release close names a ticket left open for an unchecked contract; exits non-zero when the business-rule guard failed closed; prints nothing extra without open findings; handles a pre-existing release with no tracker), `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:604-629` (a refused closure is retried at the next tick; tickets the closure could not judge are said once; a clean closure sends nothing).
