**REQ-REL-092 — Closure preview (`release contracts`): predict the closure without writing.** `release contracts --product <p> --candidate <tag> [--json] [--since <tag>] [--no-fetch]` MUST list the embarked tickets (REQ-REL-042) and judge each with the closure's own readers, **never writing to the tracker**, assigning exactly one verdict per ticket:
`unreadable` (ticket unreadable; contract unreadable in full; list could not be established) · `reopen` (cited rule(s) missing from the registry **at the candidate**; open release-blocking boxes; no declared box; contract unreadable; or post-deploy boxes that the closure cannot hand to `PostDeploy` — a duplicate's box, or a ticket not `ToDeploy`) · `post_release` (every open box is the ticket's own `## Post deploy` box and the ticket is shipped: the closure moves it to `PostDeploy`; **does not** count as `reopen` nor in the exit code) · `done` (all boxes checked) · `untouched` (already `Done`) · `mentioned` (linked to a PR of the candidate but not shipped — not `ToDeploy`/`completed`: no label, the closure does not touch it). Exit codes: **0** none would come back `InProgress`; **1** at least one `reopen`; **2** any `unreadable` (or the list/config could not be read — an unread contract never passes; a missing reviewer/angle table is unreadable config, never exit 1). Output ordered `unreadable, reopen, post_release, done, untouched, mentioned`, then by `(prefix, number)`; text report lists per ticket `<id> [<state>] @<assignee> — boxes c/t — PR <list|none> — <title>`, `→ <reason>`, each open box (prefixed `[<dup id>]` when a duplicate's) and, apart, the post-deploy boxes; JSON has `product, candidate, since, would_reopen[], to_verify_after_release[], exit_code, tickets[]`. A candidate before the cut may be a branch name. `To deploy` is judged though Linear types it `completed`; only a real `Done` is skipped.
Source: `release_contracts.py:1-37,49-128,369-590`; `tests/test_release_contracts.py:96-470` (all 29), `tests/test_release_service.py:6021-6123` (same predictions as the closure).
Notes: The preview and the closure share one function for "waits in post-release" so the prediction cannot drift from the writer (BR-AF-004).

### 2.13 Release note and sending

**REQ-REL-093 — The release item's `## Release note` section is generated from what the candidate embarks, at every candidate.** The bridge MUST write, at each candidate (with the brief, REQ-REL-046), a generated block between the markers `<!-- spl-asdlc:release-note:start -->` and `<!-- spl-asdlc:release-note:end -->` in the release item's `## Release note` section, from the chain commit → PR → ticket (REQ-REL-042):
**Aligned with the specification** (`docs/spec/01-ticket-contract.md` §6, `05-release-cycle.md` §12): a ticket is described by its `## Release note` snippet, and by the first paragraph of its Goal only when the snippet is empty; a PR no ticket links is described by the release-please changelog line that names it, else by its own summary.
- a PR that a ticket links is described by that **ticket** (`- **<id>** — <title>: <summary> (#PR, #PR)`; **a ticket of several PRs is one entry**; `(untitled)` when empty); the summary is the **first paragraph of the ticket's `Goal` section** (heading `^(?:goal|objectif|but)\s*$`, case-insensitive);
- a PR no ticket links is described by the **PR** (`- **PR #N** — <title>: <summary>`; summary = first paragraph of its `Summary|Résumé|Pourquoi|Why` section, else of its description) and is **never left out**;
- a commit no PR merged is named by its subject (`- **commit <sha12>** — <subject>`) so the note covers every change of the range;
- release bookkeeping (REQ-REL-026) is not a change and is not described;
- a ticket the tracker could not read is named with its PRs and "(ticket unreadable: content not carried over)" — never described from something else;
- sections `### Tickets` (sorted by `(prefix, number)`) and `### No linked ticket`; with neither: "No change since the last published release."; a header line stating the block is generated from `<candidate>` and **rewritten at every candidate; text outside the block is never touched**.
Paragraph extraction: split on blank lines; drop lines that start with `#`, `<!--`, `🤖`, a code fence, `|`, or `Agent:`/`Co-Authored-By:`/`Signed-off-by:` (case-insensitive); join the remaining lines of the first non-empty block with spaces; if longer than **280** characters, cut on a word boundary, strip trailing ` ,;:` and append ` …`.
`splice(description, block)`: if both markers exist (`end > start`) replace from `start` to `end` inclusive; else locate the `## Release note` heading (`^##[ \t]+Release note[ \t]*$`, case-insensitive) and: drop the fresh-issue **placeholder** line (recognised after stripping leading `\`, `_`, `*`, whatever italics the tracker hands back), trim leading/trailing blank lines, place the block **first** in the section above any human text (which stays below it), the section ending at the next `#`/`##` heading; no such heading → append `\n\n## Release note\n\n<block>\n`. `write(release, block)` MUST read the item **afresh** (a reviewer/agent may have changed it since), and write **only if the description changed**, returning whether it did. A failure to write is reported ("release note not written") and costs neither the brief nor the item: the next candidate rewrites it. Output line: `release note written | unchanged | not written`.
Source: `release_ticket_notes.py:1-183`; `linear_cli.py:573-599`; `tests/test_release_ticket_notes.py:124-241` (a ticket is described by its title and goal, an orphan PR by its description; an unread ticket is named and said unread; a ticket of several PRs is one entry; a long description is cut on a word; the PR summary skips trailers and falls back on the description; the placeholder of a fresh release issue is replaced; the next candidate rewrites the block and nothing else; text a person wrote in the section stays under the block; a description without the section gets one; `write` reads the issue afresh and skips a no-op; the placeholder is recognised whatever italics the tracker hands back).
Notes: This is the **release item's** note (what the candidate carries, for reviewers and the audit trail). It is distinct from release-please's `CHANGELOG`/GitHub-release body, which is what the GO judges (REQ-REL-023) and what is **sent** (REQ-REL-094). The Python placeholder/headers were French; English in the target.

**REQ-REL-094 — The release note is SENT through a `Notifier` when the release is published; sending is part of the deliverable.** When the bridge observes that the release's GitHub release is published (single shape: `release_tag`; multi-component: the version component's release), it MUST send **one** message through the `Notifier` trait, exactly once per published release (idempotence marker, REQ-REL-100: e.g. a comment/label on the release item `<!-- spl-asdlc:note-sent tag=<release tag> -->`), at closure, as a closure step (`docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §12; Python announced at publication). Content: a **head** — "`<Product> <version>` is published", the **GitHub release link**, the **release item** link, the **run** link — then the **release body verbatim as release-please wrote it** onto the GitHub release (never reconstructed from commits or tickets here: single canonical writer, BR-AF-004). Fixed order — version, release link, body — **so that if the channel cuts the message, only the body's tail is ever lost, never the link**. The notifier abstraction MUST expose its channel's maximum length; when `head + "\n\n" + body` exceeds it, the body MUST be cut on a **whole-line** boundary (never mid-line), a visible truncation mark ("(note truncated — full text via the release link above)") appended on its own line and never itself split, the head never truncated; if even the mark does not fit, the mark is clipped (last resort). A body with no text sends the head alone. A missing release URL falls back to "(none — manual publication by dispatch)". Syntax adaptation to the channel (escaping, bold/list rewriting) is the **notifier implementation's** duty and MUST keep the body's **text** unchanged; the length is measured in the channel's own unit (e.g. UTF-16 code units for Telegram). A send failure is a failed closure step (`docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §12): it is logged as a failure of the tick and retried at the following ticks (the marker is written only after a successful send), and the release item stays open until the note went out; it is never dropped. A tag that is not this product's (`<product>-v*`) is not announced.
**Aligned with the specification** (`docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §12): the note sent is the one the bridge builds from what was shipped (the generated block of REQ-REL-093, read at closure), not release-please's body verbatim; the head-first order, the whole-line cut and the truncation mark below are kept.
Source: `.github/workflows/release-publish.yml:379-455` ("Tell Telegram the version is published"); `src/agentforge/ci/telegram_messages.py:1-235` (`format_pypi_published_message`, truncation on a line boundary, `TRUNCATION_MARK`, `MAX_TELEGRAM_MESSAGE_LENGTH = 4096`, `NO_RELEASE_URL_FALLBACK`); `tests/test_release_gate_workflow.py:2210-2297` (publish announces its own publication; reads the release body from the GitHub release; calls the message builder; builder failure warns but does not fail the job; never fails the job; links the release and the issue; deploy announces only the install), `:2324` (BR-AF-002: closing never conditions on the deploy job).
Notes: Python sent Telegram from the CI job; the target sends from the bridge through `Notifier` (Telegram/Slack/e-mail are implementations). "Part of the deliverable" = a release whose note was never sent is **not complete** for `release status` (REQ-REL-097 reports `note_sent`). The deploy/install message ("the box installed it") is dropped (no box).

**REQ-REL-095 — Other notifications are signals, sent through the same `Notifier`, never the only record.** The bridge MUST write every one of the following as a **tracker comment** (the durable record) and MAY additionally send it through the `Notifier` **only when a human must act** (not for progress): the release-please merge announcement (REQ-REL-080); the stagnation alert (REQ-REL-079); the escalation (REQ-REL-064); the closure's "tickets left unjudged" message (REQ-REL-091); a failed note send persisting (REQ-REL-094). Messages are informational and **ask nobody to tick a verdict, merge or decide** except where the escalation says what to decide; free text MUST be escaped for the channel by the notifier, never by the caller. A notifier with no configured destination costs the notice (logged as an error, **not** marked as sent so it is retried), never the merge or the closure. Telegram topics/identities are dropped.
Source: `release_advance.py:1507-1519` (`_notify`: unconfigured topic costs only the notification), `1386-1395,1406-1477,2049-2061`; `release_qa_cli.py:281-352,419-498` (QA-done message: counts findings by severity, names a severity at zero, says when handed to a human, asks nobody to do anything, survives channel markup, never fails the run); `tests/test_release_advance.py:1743-1772` (the notifier shells out to the installed sender; reports a refused send rather than claiming success; never raises when the sender is missing; refuses an empty message) [transport dropped], `tests/test_release_qa_cli.py:351-498`.

### 2.14 Requalification

**REQ-REL-096 — A human can requalify a finding: change its severity, with a mandatory reason, recorded.** The operator verb `release requalify --version <v> --finding <ticket id | nit anchor> --severity <blocker|should-fix|nit> --reason <text|->` MUST:
- refuse an empty reason **before any write**, an unknown severity, a finding that does not belong to this release (carries no release label), and a ticket that is not a finding;
- be **idempotent**: requalifying to the severity it already has is a no-op ("already `<severity>`");
- to `blocker`: write the marker block (REQ-REL-056) into the description and lay the `blocks` relation (REQ-REL-057/058; a relation failure raises `FindingNotLinked` semantics — the marker is there, reconciliation will lay it); the GO becomes false at the next tick;
- from `blocker` to `should-fix`/`nit`: remove the `blocks` relation **and** the marker line (the marker is otherwise "do not remove by hand"), keep the release label, add `qa-finding` (for `should-fix`), set `Triage`; for `nit`, move the finding to the nits work (REQ-REL-061) and cancel the ticket;
- from a nit line to `should-fix`/`blocker`: create the ticket via REQ-REL-052 and tick/strike the nit line;
- **always** post a comment on the finding: who (the requalifying operator, as the tracker reports it), from → to, and the reason, and a one-line note on the release item so the audit trail shows why the GO changed; never silently edit severity.
The verb is **the only sanctioned way to withdraw a blocker** other than fixing and closing it; the bridge itself never lowers a severity. Requalifying a blocker to non-blocking while the release's GO was true/false changes nothing until the next tick recomputes it.
Source: target design (no Python verb); closest Python: `release_service.py:182-199` (the blocker marker contract "do not remove by hand: close the finding"), `2704-2780`, `linear_cli.py:887-947` (`release finding --severity blocker|should-fix|nit`), `release_qa.py:99-107` (`RejectedFinding.reason` mandatory).
Notes: The reason-mandatory rule is the Python "rejection without a reason fails closed" invariant carried to the human path.

### 2.15 Status and tracker writes

**REQ-REL-097 — Operator verbs.** `spl-asdlc release …` MUST provide, read-mostly and with the semantics of the REQs cited:
`status [--version v] [--candidate tag] [--gate green|red|unknown] [--require-go] [--json]` — computes the GO (REQ-REL-066/067) after reconciliation (REQ-REL-058; the one write this read verb may make); `--candidate` supplies the note (REQ-REL-023), fix (REQ-REL-059) and tip (REQ-REL-070) conditions — **without it neither the note nor the fixes are judged** (the publication's own call); `--gate` defaults to `unknown` which is **never green**; output `release: <id>`, `go: yes|no`, `blockers-carried: <value>`, `reconciled: …`, indented reasons; `--json` prints `GoDecision.as_dict()` plus `reconciled[]`; `--require-go` exits 1 unless GO; no release item → exit 1 "no release issue for `<product>` `<version>`".
`status` (narrated; the audio facade's semantics): for every **open** cycle: `product, release, url, version, candidate, stage, tracker state, gate colour (REQ-REL-035), angles {rendered, missing}, go, waiting_for_human, findings (REQ-REL-062 or absent), next_action`; with no open cycle: `last_published {version, published_at}` per product from the highest final tag of the shape's release prefix (`v<version>`, commit date) and the stage "no cycle open: the last published version is the current state". `waiting_for_human` is true iff open/missing angles, blockers, note gaps, or fix gaps exist (a **green GO is not** on that list: the merge is the bridge's). **Stages** (derived, never recomputed): 1 cut (no readable candidate yet) · 2 gate (not green: "the gate is `<colour>`: `<detail>`") · 3 review (angles outstanding) · 4 go refused (reasons) · 5 go green (the bridge merges by itself — "it recomputes the GO at the moment it advances, with the graph reconciliation this status cannot do, so it can still refuse"). Publication/closure (6, 7) are NOT derived for an open cycle (a stale clone would say "published" about a cycle awaiting merge); 7 is narrated only once no cycle is open. Unknown product → refuse naming the configured ones. Status MUST include `note_sent` once published (REQ-REL-094).
`classify` (REQ-REL-011; no network, no tracker; one TAB line) · `template` (print the release item and angle sub-item bodies that would be created; no network, no identity; a missing angle config is a config error) · `brief` (REQ-REL-044) · `contracts` (REQ-REL-092) · `close --version v` (REQ-REL-084..091, the manual re-run; exits non-zero when `rules_unverified`) · `requalify` (REQ-REL-096) · `post-release-check` (REQ-REL-090; **not carried**: corrected by the specification, the checkbox mechanism replaces it) · `verify-note` (REQ-REL-025).
Source: `linear_cli.py:721-807,253-332,602-884,970-1172` (`_add_release_parsers`); `release_audio.py:124-348`; `tests/test_linear_cli.py:1048-1109` (status refuses the GO for a candidate whose note omits commits; with a covering note is a GO; without a candidate does not read the note; refuses the GO for a candidate lacking a blocker's fix; without a candidate does not read the fixes), `tests/test_release_service.py:3894-3950` (every release verb is reachable from the CLI; the status verb defaults to an unknown gate; the template verb needs no identity; without angle config is a config error; a verb without an identity exits rather than guessing), `tests/test_release_audio.py:78-410,520-560` (all `release_status` tests; the protocol-text tests are dropped).
Notes: Python verbs `preflight`, `open`, `verdict`, `finding` (the agent-facing one) are dropped/replaced (§3).

**REQ-REL-098 — The exact tracker operations the chain needs (the `Tracker` trait surface for the release area).** An implementation of `Tracker` for Linear or Livela MUST provide the following; the **write** set below is exhaustive for this area. Item fields: `id`, `key` (identifier), `title`, `description`, `url`, `state` (name) + `state_kind`, `team`, `labels[]`, `assignee`, `children[]`, `relations` (`blocks` in/out with relation ids; `related`; `duplicate`), `comments[]` (author, body; `author_is_bridge`), `priority`.
Reads: (R1) `find_by_label_and_title(label, title)` (≤200, warn when a full page); (R2) `list_open_by_title_prefix_and_marker(prefix, marker)`; (R3) `get(key, wide=false|true)` (wide = full comment/history window for the author/reassign decision); (R4) `list_by_label(label)` (≤200) returning at least key, id, title, state+kind, labels, description, blocked-by arcs `(blocker key, relation id)`, assignee; (R5) `team_key(team) → "SID"|""` (never raises); (R6) `states(team)` resolving a logical state to an id, **failing** when the team lacks it; (R7) `tickets_linked_to_pr(url) → keys[]` (attachments/links; error is an error, REQ-REL-042); (R8) `duplicates(item)` closure walk (bounded; exhaustion reported); (R9) `blockers_of(item)` for the cycle walk (REQ-REL-057); (R10) `auto_closes_children(team)`; (R11) `rework_assignee(item) → (user?, reason)`; (R12) `assignee_name(item)`.
Writes: (W1) `ensure_label(team, name) → id` (create if absent); (W2) `create_item{team, title, description, labels, state, priority, assignee?, parent?}` (atomic; return id/key/url) — used for the release item, angle sub-items, blocker/should-fix tickets, the nits ticket; (W3) `update_description(item, text)` (nit append, release-note block, box tick, requalification); (W4) `add_label(item, label) → bool` (false when the team lacks it); `remove_label`; (W5) `set_state(item, state, assignee?, priority?)` — one write when state and assignee change together; (W6) `comment(item, body)`; (W7) `relate(a, b, blocks|related)` with REQ-REL-057 refusal semantics; (W8) `unrelate(relation id)` (non-retried; empty id refuses); (W9) `assign(item, user)` (escalation); (W10) `link_pr(item, pr_url)` (optional, when the backend can attach).
Operation → REQ: release item REQ-REL-039 (R1,R6,W1,W2); candidate comment 041 (W6,W3); embarked tickets/labels 042-043 (R7,R5,R3,W4); brief 044-046 (W6); angle sub-items 048 (W2,W6); findings 052-053/060-061 (R4,W1,W2,W7,W5,W3); blockers 056-058 (R4,W2,W7); resolved-blocker proof 059 (R4,R3 comments); escalation 064 (W9,W4,W6,R3); HOLD 069 (W6); closure 084-091 (R4,R3,R8,R10,R11,W5,W6,W4,W3); post-deploy verb 090 (R3,W3,W5,W6,R8); release note 093 (R3,W3); requalify 096 (W3,W4,W7,W8,W5,W6).
Source: `linear_service.py` callers across `release_service.py`, `release_contracts.py`, `release_ticket_notes.py` (e.g. `release_service.py:2078-2104` `ensure_label`; `linear_service.py:3780-3891` `_create_issue`/`_relate`/`_unrelate`).
Notes: `Tracker` MUST NOT leak backend vocabulary into the release logic; the **logical state names** (`ToDeploy`, `PostDeploy`, …) are the exact canonical names in a tracker (Linear: `To Deploy`, `Post Deploy`). An adapter whose backend uses IDs maps those IDs internally. This supersedes the former Linear display-name mapping. (R11) `rework_assignee` is not carried (REQ-REL-089 as corrected): the closure's reopening clears the assignee in its status write (`set_status(key, InProgress, Some(None))`; Linear `issueUpdate` with `assigneeId: null`).

**REQ-REL-099 — The GitHub operations the chain needs.** Reads: tags (list/sort/exists/annotation kind and body/creator date/commit); commits in a range (sha, subject); `is_ancestor`; branches containing a ref (`origin/release/*`); remote heads by pattern (with exit status); file at ref (`CHANGELOG.md`, `release-please-config.json`, registry, record, report); changed files between refs; open PRs (≤30: number, title, body, head ref, head sha, base, author login + is_bot, labels, draft, mergeable, mergeStateStatus, checks rollup, url); merged PRs by base (≤500, labels, title); `commits/{sha}/pulls`; PR files (`pulls/{n}/files`); PR view (`state, mergeCommit`); release view (exists vs not found vs error); workflow runs of a workflow (≤100: headSha, headBranch, event, conclusion, url, databaseId) and a run's jobs; artifact download (`release-gate-evidence-<sha>`); review state of a PR (approvals by login on the head SHA). Writes: `pr merge --squash --match-head-commit`; `pr update-branch` (record PR and angle PR **only**, never a PR whose head is `release/**`); `pr create`; create/delete ref; `PUT contents`; `workflow run` (release-please redispatch; cut `candidate`); `workflow rerun-failed-jobs` (CI-side). All GitHub calls MUST run under the bridge's single GitHub identity; a call that fails is a failed read/write named in the reason, never a silent default.
Source: `release_advance.py` (`_gh`, `_gh_full` call sites throughout), `release_contracts.py:199-238`, `release_fixes.py:57-77`, `release_qa_candidate.py:98-165`.

**REQ-REL-100 — Idempotence keys and bridge state.** The chain's once-only behaviours MUST be idempotent across ticks **and across restarts**: (a) HOLD said — `(candidate, sorted blockers+fix_gaps)` list; (b) stagnation reported — `candidate`; (c) release-please redispatched — `candidate`; (d) undeployed hold said — `(candidate, sorted component#pr)` list; (e) escalation — `candidate` (marker in the comment, REQ-REL-064); (f) brief posted, candidate comment posted, note block written — `candidate`; (g) note sent — `release tag`; (h) angle sub-item/finding ticket exists — REQ-REL-053; (i) `candidate` cut dispatched — `(cycle, N)`; (j) closure — the member states themselves. A mark MUST be written **after** the effect it records (a failed mark costs a duplicate, never a lost action — "of the two, the duplicate is the one that still tells somebody"), except where a duplicate is itself harmful (REQ-REL-072 marks the redispatch at merge time). A write that fails to persist a mark never fails the tick. Marks SHOULD live where they survive a bridge on another machine — as hidden `<!-- spl-asdlc:… -->` markers on the release item/PR, queried on read — and MAY be cached in local state (§5). The action journal (REQ-REL-080) is append-only.
Source: `release_advance.py:1259-1332,2586-2652,1980-2046`; `release_service.py:111-115,795-814`; `release_qa_cli.py:241-258` (marker present ⇒ not re-posted); `tests/test_release_advance.py:1327-1364,1442,1484`, `tests/test_release_advance_cycle.py:673-694`.
Notes: Python used `data/*.json` files under the instance root (corrupt/unreadable file → treated as empty, with a warning) — a corrupt store MUST read as empty and warn, never crash.

**REQ-REL-101 — Configuration surface in `spl-asdlc.toml`.** The keys, their defaults and their fail-closed behaviour are normative in `docs/spec/08-configuration.md` (`[product]`, `[owner]`, `[tracker]`, `[release]`, `[[release.angles]]`, `[review]`, `[bridge]`, `[[notify]]`). The release chain reads: the shape (`candidate_tag`, `release_tag`, `components`, `version_component`, `gate_record`, `release_please_authors`, `release_pr_label`; REQ-REL-027/028), `branch_prefix` (stamp format fixed, REQ-REL-004), `record` (angle reports), `business_rule_paths`, `registry_path`, `bookkeeping_paths` (REQ-REL-070), `max_rc_passes`, `later_passes` (REQ-REL-054), `go_policy`, `stagnation_hours`, `coordinators`, `escalation_label`, `auto_candidate` (REQ-REL-065), the workflow names, the owner (escalation) and the notification channels. The identities among them (`product.repository`, the owner, `coordinators`, `release_please_authors`, angle assignees, the channels) may come from the instance overlay instead (spec 08, "The instance overlay"); a key set in both files refuses the repo, never overrides (BR-ASDLC-014). The release class reads the repository's sensitive-path file (`[review] sensitive_paths`, REQ-REL-011). Unreadable or incoherent config **refuses the repo for the tick** with a named reason (never defaults a safety setting, and a present but invalid value is an error, never replaced by its default). There are no per-product overrides (one repo = one product).
Source: `release_shape.py:17-37`; `release_service.py:305-352,394-493`; `release_qa_launch.py:47-68`; `release_advance.py:158,1499-1504`.

**REQ-REL-102 — Cross-cutting rules.** (a) **Never raise out of a tick for one repo**; (b) every tracker/GitHub/git read that fails is a named reason, never a default; (c) **logging**: refusals that switch a guard off or on (`registry absent`, `team id missing`, `fetch failed`) are logged at **WARNING or above** (a lower level was never seen by the Python CLI path); (d) `Decision`s and results are reported with `repo`, `candidate`, `release`, `action`, `reason`, `detail`; (e) tracker text is English; (f) a candidate/version string is always normalised through one function (`0.4.90` ≡ `v0.4.90`); (g) an identifier never appears as a bare token where a link is available (tracker items and PRs are rendered as links by the notifier/tracker implementation).
Source: `release_advance.py:2772-2804`; `release_service.py:499-527,3652-3664`; `release_advance.py:2807-2824`.

## Dropped from the Python (with reason)

Each item is something the Python does that the target does **not** carry, and why. "Replaced" points at the REQ that takes over the intent.

### 3.1 Fleet, identities, host and transport

| Dropped | Reason |
|---|---|
| `resolve_python`, `AGENTFORGE_PYTHON`, `RELEASE_ROOT`, `resolve_release_root`, the `sudo -n -u <owner>` prefix, `release_cli` indirection, "No Python with the agentforge package" refusal, the instance-root readability dance (`cut-rc.sh:646-722`, `linear_cli._release_identity`, `release_service.resolve_release_auth`, `load_fleet_env`, `_release_auth`) | No box paths, no `sudo`, no instance, no agent identities. The bridge holds one tracker credential and one GitHub identity from its own config. |
| `release_reviewers.json` / `load_release_config` / `release_config_path` / `release_agent` / `post_release_agent` / `linear_user_ids` / `release_user_ids` / `post_release_assignee_id` / `products.<p>` overrides / `_product_overrides` / `scope_drift` / `DOCUMENTED_SCOPES` (the design scope of one repository) | Vault tables are gone; angles and escalation are `spl-asdlc.toml` (REQ-REL-047, 101). One config = one repo = one product, so per-product overrides and "documented scope vs table drift" have no meaning. |
| Agent @mentions that wake an agent (BR-AF-062, a past incident) in reopen/closure/hold comments; `agent_mention`, `load_status_routing`, the `unassigned` routing | There is no heartbeat and no sleeping agent to wake. The tracker's own assignee/notification model carries the hand-off (a reopened ticket is unassigned and picked up by role, REQ-REL-089). |
| `release_advance.RELEASE_PLEASE_AUTHORS` (`<service-app>[bot]`, `app/<service-app>`), `RELEASE_PLEASE_AUTHOR` | Hard-coded service identity of one installation; replaced by `[release].release_please_authors` (REQ-REL-027/030). |
| Telegram: `notify.send_telegram`, `notify.resolve_topic`, `_notify`, `qa_hold_notified.json`, `_notify_qa_hold`, `escape_legacy_markdown` call sites, the `ci-autorelease notify` wrapper and its topic allowlist, the gate-green/gate-red/rerun-call-failed Telegram jobs, the QA-done message (`qa_done_message`, `announce_qa_done`) | No Telegram. Signals go through the `Notifier` trait (REQ-REL-094/095). Progress messages (gate green, QA done) are not signals: the tracker comment is the record. |
| `data/*.json` marker files (`release_stagnation.json`, `release_please_redispatch.json`, `release_hold_notes.json`, `release_undeployed_notes.json`, `qa_hold_notified.json`) under `resolve_root`, and `pr_merge.append_action` (`data/action_log.jsonl`) | Instance-root paths. Replaced by REQ-REL-100 (marks recoverable from tracker/GitHub) and REQ-REL-080 (journal). |
| `pr_merge.discover_repos`, `product_checkouts`, `_product_repo_dir`, `canonical_repo_slug` rename heuristics (a past incident/827), `declared_shape` matching a *checkout directory name*, "two products claim one checkout" | One repo per config; nothing to discover. The *declared release tag* part of a past incident is kept (REQ-REL-088). |
| `_archive_before_open` / `linear_archive` (Free-plan quota) | Tracker-plan housekeeping; a quota refusal is an ordinary create failure (REQ-REL-039). |
| The heartbeat/ordering tests ("the pass runs after the merge pass"), `run_pass`, `main()` CLI of `release_advance`, `emergency_stop.sh` | No heartbeat. The bridge's tick is the only driver (REQ-REL-068/071). |
| `GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`/`GH_TOKEN` env handling of `_fetch_tags` | Transport detail of the Python subprocesses; the "never prompt" requirement is restated in REQ-REL-031. |

| Dropped | Reason / replacement |

| Legacy per-reviewer QA sub-issues (`qa_title`, `_create_qa_child`, `scope_decision`/`add_candidate` sub-issue path, `ensure_template`'s legacy five-issue repair) | Pre-a past incident release shape; never exists in the target. The reopen/still-open/kept **decision** is kept (REQ-REL-054). |

Ticking tracker boxes "from what the code constated" is moot with no tracker box. |
| The **post-release child** and its machinery: `POST_RELEASE_TITLE`, `BLOCKED_BY_RELEASE_LABEL`, `_create_post_release_child`, `unblocked_post_release`, `interlocked_qa`, `_announce_withdrawn_release_arc`, the two extra arms of `reconcile_release`, `Reconciliation.withdrawn/unblocked_post_release`, `team_auto_closes_children` (as a precondition) | Each embarked ticket carries its own `## Post deploy` boxes and moves to `PostDeploy` (REQ-REL-087/090); no release child waits for deployment. |

What survives as a **contract** is the report schema and its fail-closed rules (REQ-REL-050), `max_rc_passes` and escalation (REQ-REL-063/064), and the evidence-pack facts (REQ-REL-037). |

The **semantics** of `release_status` (stage derivation, gate colour allowlist, `waiting_for_human`, findings counts, `last_published`) are REQ-REL-035/062/097. |

### 3.3 Cut and workflow plumbing

| Dropped | Reason |
|---|---|
| `release_cli preflight`, the "No Linear identity — refusing to cut" refusal, the **local-tag-then-issue-then-push** order and its unwinding (`git tag -d`), `--candidate-commit` capability detection (`release open --help`) | Reversed ordering: the cut never touches the tracker (REQ-REL-016). The version-skew guard existed because the installed engine could be older than the script. |
| Fallback to `pyproject.toml`'s version on the `candidate` path, "dry run: the version comes from pyproject" wording | Replaced by "reuse the cycle's newest RC version, else refuse" (REQ-REL-007, open question). |
| "Round > 2 informational note" | Replaced by enforced `max_rc_passes` (REQ-REL-063/064). |
| `CUT_RC_POLL_INTERVAL` env | Poll interval is config (REQ-REL-006). |
| `AGENTFORGE_RUNNER_READY` variable, self-hosted/hosted runner fallbacks, `release.yml` preflight about the runner, the `fresh-env`, GPU-sharing, shared-user-site (a past incident) and `usersite` guard workflow tests | Box/runner infrastructure of one installation. |
| `ci-release` / `ci-autorelease` root-owned wrappers (verbs `status`, `close`, `verdict`, `finding`, `notify`, `deploy`, `rollback`, `verify`, `cut`, `open`) and their allowlists | `sudo`/box wrappers; the bridge replaces `status`/`close`; `deploy`/`rollback`/`verify` are the box install (below). |
| `publish-pypi`'s "Verify the computed GO" step (which called the tracker), `release-status-dry-run` advisory job, `release-brief` job, `notify-gate-failure` fix-task job (the chain agent task), `close-release` job's tracker calls, `closing-guard` | CI never writes to (or reads from) a tracker in the target; the bridge computes the GO, posts the brief and closes. The `notify-gate-failure` fix task is replaced by the bridge's **gate-fix ticket** (owner's decision, 2026-10-08; `docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §3, BR-ASDLC-016): once a candidate's gate is red and its automatic rerun settled (or none showed up within `release.gate_rerun_wait_minutes`), the bridge opens one urgent, unassigned gate-fix ticket in Ready under the release issue, carrying the failing suites of `results/suites.json` (or saying they are unknown) and the gate run; the ticket then follows the flow of any ticket, and the bridge closes it once a newer candidate's gate is green only when none of its PRs merged. The CI-side QA'd-commit guard (`Verify this release was QA'd`) and `verify-note` stay (REQ-REL-070/025). |
| `deploy` job: install the published version on the box, verify units restarted after install, roll back to `previous_version`, the `jq` filters of the verify step (`tests/test_release_publish_jq_filters.sh`), install/rollback Telegram messages | Box deployment of a Python package. Not a release-chain requirement of the bridge. |
| `merge-back` job runner-fallback tests, `ci-autorelease` branch-name quirk of `%(refname:strip=3)` as a *script* behaviour | CI mechanics; the **contracts** the bridge relies on are in REQ-REL-017. |

### 3.4 Language and policy artefacts

| Dropped | Reason |
|---|---|
| All French strings (release item, brief, comments, reason sentences, stage labels), `🤖` prefixes | Everything written to the tracker/GitHub/notifier is English (REQ-REL-067 restates the reasons). |
| `_RELEASE_BLOCKER_RE` French human text inside the marker block; `Aucune nouvelle règle.` (`ABSENCE_DECLARATION`) handling for the **merge-time** business-rule guard (`declares_new_rule`, `new_rule_statement`) | Only the closure-time half (cited ids exist at the tag) is in scope for `release`; the merge-time "unpromoted rule" guard belongs to the PR/ticket area. |
| Priority/budget commentary (`starred`, token-budget `critical` tier, `_is_release_gate_child_shape`) | Token-budget gate of the fleet. Priority **Urgent** is kept as data (REQ-REL-039/048). |
| `docs/RELEASE_MANUAL.md` "no line-number citations" rule (`tests/test_release_manual_citations.py`) | Documentation hygiene of the Python repo; the Rust docs SHOULD follow the same rule (cite by name, not line) but it is not a bridge behaviour. |
| `parse_tickets` free-text identifier harvesting (`_TICKET_RE`) | Forbidden by BR-AF-083 (REQ-REL-042). Its team-key idea survives only as `team_key_of(identifier)` on the release item's own identifier. |

## Test cases to carry over

Scope: every test function of `tests/test_release_*.py` (15 files incl. the QA-engine files), `tests/test_verify_release_notes.py`, `tests/test_gate_auto_rerun.py`, `tests/test_release_manual_citations.py`, and the release parts of `tests/test_linear_cli.py` (the two `queue-diag` tests at lines 37 and 56 are not release tests and are excluded). `tests/test_release_publish_jq_filters.sh` (4 shell cases pinning `jq` filters of the box `deploy` verify step) is **dropped** with the box deploy (§3.3). `tests/release_reviewers_table.py` is a fixture builder for the vault reviewer table (dropped with it); `tests/fixtures/release_publish_runs_2026_09.json` belongs to the publish-run filters (dropped).

**850 tests total: 626 carry over (as behaviour to re-implement as Rust tests; the Python test's inputs and edge cases are the fixtures), 224 are dropped with a reason.** "Carry" means the *behaviour* is required by the REQ named; the Python test body is a reference for fixtures, not for structure. `test_release_manual_citations.py` pins one thing: `docs/RELEASE_MANUAL.md` cites code by step/function/branch name and never by line number (a past incident) — dropped as a documentation rule of the Python repo (the Rust docs SHOULD follow it).

French assertions in the Python tests (reason sentences, comments) become English golden strings fixed once (open question 20).

## Open questions

1. **Report format and location (REQ-REL-050).** The Python finding schema (`release-finding-schema.md`) lives in the vault and is not in the repository; the JSON schema here is **reconstructed** from `release_qa.py` (`verdict`, mandatory `checked`, `findings[{title, severity, body, role, sources}]`, `rejected[{id, reason}]`) and the `blocker | should-fix | nit` vocabulary. Decide: JSON file vs Markdown with front-matter; path (`qa-reports/<candidate>/<angle>.json`); whether `status`, `fixed_in`, `test_added` are the right fields for "finding schema, verdict, test added".
2. **Who cuts `rc.N+1` (REQ-REL-065).** The target says "a new RC is tagged". Does the bridge dispatch `release.yml action=candidate` itself (needs a token that can dispatch workflows), or does the coordinator/human? And how does a human authorise a pass beyond `max_rc_passes` (raise the config, a label on the release item, a one-shot command)?
3. **(Settled: `later_passes`, REQ-REL-054.) `qa_scope = "diff"` (REQ-REL-054).** The target says full QA on every RC by default. Is the Python "reopen only the reviews whose scope changed" kept as an opt-in, or dropped entirely? (It is specified as opt-in; every scope test would be dropped if not.)
4. **Coordinator approval semantics (REQ-REL-051).** GitHub review `APPROVED` by a configured login on the current head SHA? A label? Is the coordinator an agent or a human? Who performs the merge (the bridge, as written, or the coordinator)? The Python consolidation invariant — *every role finding is accounted for exactly once; a rejection needs a reason; a consolidation that drops a blocker fails closed* — has no GitHub-native equivalent; REQ-REL-050 approximates it with `status`/`reason` rules in the report. Is that enough?
5. **Where idempotence marks live (REQ-REL-100).** Hidden markers on the release item/PR (survives a bridge on another machine, costs a tracker read) vs a local state store (fast, lost with the machine). Can two bridges run at once? The irreversible actions are safe (`--match-head-commit`, GitHub refuses a second merge); creates are made idempotent by label+title; but comments (HOLD, stagnation) could duplicate.
6. **`candidate` path with no release-please PR (REQ-REL-007).** Python falls back to the manifest version (the version already *published*). Target proposal: reuse the version of the newest RC of this cycle, else refuse. Confirm.
7. **Closure with `rules_unverified` (REQ-REL-091).** Python closes the release item anyway and notifies once because nothing re-runs the closure. Target proposal: keep the release item open and retry every tick, notify after `stagnation_hours`. Confirm, or keep Python's behaviour.
8. **Gate record for single-component products (REQ-REL-078).** Python writes `docs/releases/v<X>.md` (with `Blockers-Carried: none`) only for multi-component shapes, because a multi-component project's deploy refuses without it. Should the bridge offer the record for every shape (the AgentForge publish workflow read `Blockers-Carried` from an existing record and reconciled it with the tracker)?
9. **Placement of the gate-rerun decision (REQ-REL-037).** Keep `gate_auto_rerun.py`/`known-flakes.json` as CI scripts, or port the decision to `spl-asdlc ci gate-rerun-decision`? The rule is the contract either way; `known-flakes.json` format (`suite`, `ticket`, `expires`, `note`) is unchanged.
10. **(Settled: `[release].nits`, REQ-REL-061.) Nit grouping and `qa-finding` (REQ-REL-052/060/061).** Keep the PM decision "a nit never gets its own ticket; one shared `Nits — <product> v<version>` ticket" and the `qa-finding` label name? Does Livela have per-item checkboxes (the shared-ticket mechanism relies on Markdown checkbox lines in a description)?
11. **Trust anchor for resolved-blocker fixes (REQ-REL-059).** Python trusts `merged_pr::` markers written under a *fleet identity* (`fleet_author_ids`). With no agent identities: trust only comments authored by the bridge's tracker identity, or rely on the tracker's native PR link? Does Livela expose PR attachments for `tickets_linked_to_pr` (REQ-REL-042/098 R7) and **atomic create-with-relations** (REQ-REL-056)?
12. **Git access mode.** Is a local clone (tags fetched, `merge-base`, `git log`, `git show <ref>:<path>`) assumed, or must the bridge use only the GitHub API (`compare`, `git/refs`, `contents?ref=`)? The ancestor test (`contains`, `is_ancestor`), the range (`<tag>..<candidate>`), the branch-containing-ref query (`branch -r --contains`) and the "file at ref" reads all have API equivalents with different limits (e.g. compare caps at 250 commits).
13. **Release-note sending (REQ-REL-094).** Moment: at publication (as Python) or after closure? Is `note_sent` a precondition of "complete"? Does the multi-component product send one note per component or one for the version component? Is the Telegram-specific truncation/escaping a notifier concern only (as written)?
14. **`deployed: production` for single-component products (REQ-REL-082).** Multi-component closure reads the label that each component's deploy job sets (BR-HC-152). Is there an equivalent for single-component products, or is "release published + merge-back merged" the whole trigger (as written)?
15. **First release of a product with no previous final tag (REQ-REL-018).** Python refuses to read the whole history. Does the target need a bootstrap (explicit `since`), as the brief already falls back to the earliest candidate?
16. **Manual finding filing.** Python had an agent-facing `release finding` verb. With reports as the only source, is a human `release finding` (file a blocker outside any angle PR) wanted besides `requalify`?
17. **Tip-equals-RC rule and bookkeeping set (REQ-REL-070).** New in the target (it was a CI-only publication guard). The bookkeeping path list is product-specific (release-please manifest, changelog, version files, the per-RC record `docs/releases/<rc>.md`); confirm it belongs in `spl-asdlc.toml` and that the GO (not only CI) should enforce it.
18. **Tracker state names.** `ToDeploy`/`PostDeploy` are Linear columns in the Python; confirm the Livela equivalents and the state *kinds* the trait must expose (REQ-REL-098).
19. **Angle sub-items per RC vs per release (REQ-REL-048).** Python's legacy per-reviewer sub-issues were per release; the target text says "for each configured angle the bridge creates a sub-item" and QA runs on every RC. Specified as one sub-item per angle **per RC pass** (title carries `rc.N`); alternatively one per angle per release, reopened on each RC. Which fits Livela's item model better?
20. **English reason strings as golden text.** REQ-REL-067 fixes the English reasons; tests that assert French substrings in the Python (`la gate CI n'est pas verte`, `verdicts QA non rendus`…) need an English golden set decided once.



## Editor's decisions on the open questions (2026-10-05)

- REQ-REL-070 (latest RC equals the release-branch tip modulo bookkeeping): **kept** — it is the "staged version equals the judged version" condition of `docs/spec/05-release-cycle.md` §8, now checked by the bridge rather than by the publication workflow only.
- REQ-REL-007 (reuse the cycle's newest RC version when no release-please PR exists): **kept**.
- REQ-REL-091 (keep the release item open while a ticket's business-rule check could not run): **kept** — fail-closed, `docs/spec/02-business-rules.md` §6.
- REQ-REL-082 (report a published release that was not merged back or closed, instead of filing a blocking issue from CI): **kept** — CI never writes to a tracker.
- REQ-REL-065 (the bridge dispatches `candidate` itself): **kept as a per-project policy**, default off: the coordinator tags the next RC; `[release] auto_candidate = true` lets the bridge do it.
- REQ-REL-054 (narrowing the angles on later passes): **kept as opt-in**, `later_passes = "gate-and-coordinator"`; full QA is the default.
- REQ-REL-050 (angle report schema): superseded by `docs/spec/06-qa-report.md`, which is the normative schema.
- Open question 18 (state names): the canonical names are "To Deploy" and "Post Deploy" on every tracker (`docs/spec/03-vocabulary.md`); the adapter maps, and Livela adopts the same names.
